Fuck! Most of my creations was sabotaged. Even though I had most of them resemble design flaws they would most likely be discovered. Best case was that they would merely be corrected, but they might also be identified as actual sabotage. And in that case then I was well and truly fucked. And not the kind of fucked that I preferred.

I could just barely through my dimming vision see Varl gathering up his attendants and giving orders.

Then I passed the fuck out.


---

## Chapter 20 — CHAPTER TWENTY Trial {#chapter-20}

CHAPTER TWENTY

Trial

I woke up some time later in darkness, cold and shivering. Instinctively I raised a shield and cast a heating spell but instead I promptly threw up again when the spells misfired. My stomach was empty and all I got was painful dry heaves. I had forgotten my magical fetters. Slowly I uncurled from the ball of misery that I had become. At least the bindings were not keeping me trussed up like a prize hog. Unluckily for me Varl had added his own touch to his fetters and unlike the kind the Bizhjerai employed these ones suppressed my magesense. I was well and truly blinded for the first time in a decade and the realisation sent icy fingers of dread down my spine. I didn't know if it was dark because my cell lacked windows or if it was night time. I had no recollection of a cell such as this being built so either it had been constructed by Zandor and his teams of builders or it had been built while I was out cold. I forced myself onto aching limbs and stood up only to promptly bash the top of my head on the ceiling. I collapsed with a curse and rubbed at my scalp. It was incredibly rare for me to ever strike my head against ceilings. If not even I could stand up straight in here then this cell was tiny indeed. I tried to stretch out to my full height and my boots struck wall far sooner than I had expected. I pushed against the wall, sliding myself across the floor slowly, until my scalp brushed the opposite wall. I rotated myself and repeated the experiment in the other direction. I was in a cube that was a good bit shorter than I was. Slightly more confident about the dimensions of my prison I sat up and crossed my legs.

Now what?

I patted myself down. I still wore my Angel armour, though the main engines and the wings had been removed. When I reached over my shoulder I could feel the jagged ends of the mounting and I knew that they had been forcibly removed. I couldn't access my power and my weapons were gone. Trying to not let panic creep in I wondered what I was supposed to do when nature called. I hadn't encountered a bucket in my blind exploration and I really didn't want to make a mess of my tiny prison. If I did and it was not cleaned before I had to sleep then I was likely to roll into my own filth. I shuddered at the thought.

Still in an attempt to stave the creeping dread off I tried to plan for what to say when they came to question me. My cover was obviously blown, so I had to figure out a new story that would exonerate me of any crimes but that also made sense with my old story. Why would a Skylander lie about where they were trained in Construction Magic? Why would they use Mathias Varl as the foundation of their lie?

There were only one answer to those questions. Because they had been trained by the enemy, and that fact itself would burn any trust that I had managed to establish. It took years to be as proficient in Construction Magic as I was and the only place where I could have received such training was either with a Skylander, in which case then why would I lie? Or I had been trained in Dun Traelak. The Skylanders wouldn't trust anyone that had been that deep into the heart of the enemy for that long. I waited patiently for my mind to present a neat solution. Then I waited some more. With growing trepidation, the time trickled by. I came up with nothing that could get me out of this situation. The darkness and silence was getting to me, slowly sifting through my thoughts until my mind became a still pool of nothing. A calm surface disguising a roiling vortex of growing dread. I don't know how long I sat in that uncomfortable position, shivering from cold and slowly losing my mind. I might have drifted off. I might have stayed awake. I have no way of knowing. All I know is that when the monotony was broken it was so sudden and jarring that I nearly passed out from a mix of fright and relief that there were still light somewhere in the world. I blinked into the blinding light, wincing away from the painful rays. My eyes watered and tears trickled down my cheeks. It was sunlight. I tried to crawl out into the warmth but my legs had seized up during my long vigil and I fell on my side, crushing my shoulder painfully.

"Get her out of there," a voice commanded.

I heard the rustle of cloth before a large hand closed around my collar to haul me out into the sun. I just lay there, luxuriating in the sensation of fully stretching and being rid of that horrible cold. I kept my eyes closed and just rested. A boot struck my ribs but my armoured corset absorbed most of the shock.

"Get up," that same harsh voice ordered.

I managed to struggle onto my knees. My vision was clearing slowly and I squinted at whoever had been speaking. I couldn't make out his face but the bright mop of pink hair on the top of his head was unmistakable. Micah. And that tall and thin shade behind him had to be Varl.

"Do it, Mathias," Micah told his compatriot and the shade stepped closer. He raised his hands and the blue glow of magic surrounded the limb. I could see it much clearer than anything else and to my horror he devoured my armour. He ripped the magic straight out of the composite material until it melted and hissed down my body in rivulets before evaporating. I was shocked to my core. I had only ever heard of a single being that was powerful enough to destroy materialised magic like that. Mother. After her fight with Grasax the liche. The battle that had ravaged her body but transformed her Soul into a magical powerhouse. It had been forced to adapt, or burn, and she had just barely managed to avoid going full Archon.

Apparently Mathias was not only a brilliant Construction Mage, he was also a supremely powerful Mage. My armour dripped and melted but Mathias didn't stop there. My blouse evaporated in puffs and spouts before my bra started to follow suit.

"That's enough," Micah finally intervened.

I almost sobbed in gratitude. Even though I was not prude and didn't mind people seeing me naked this forced undressing was humiliating. I didn't mind people seeing me when I chose it. But this... This was something else.

"See, Micah?" Varl hissed. "It's the same as the other. These markings are much too complex to be random."

The other? The only other person I knew of that had the same kind of tattoo all over their arm and shoulder was Alissa. With a sudden lurch my world twisted. They had captured Alissa. Aunt Alissa.

How? She was a master of subterfuge. How had they managed to capture her? And when? How long had I been in that cell?

If Alissa had been caught and they knew who she was then they would never let her go. They'd kill her, if they hadn't already. And now that they had linked the two of us together they would certainly kill me as well. And I was powerless to stop them. My magic was suppressed and even if I could lay my hands on a weapon the magical fetters around my limbs could hogtie me with a short trigger word. I was useless.

Micah stared down at me disdainfully.

"Who are you?" He muttered before shaking his head. "Put her back in the cell," he snapped at a soldier and turned to leave.

"No!" I yelled after him. "Don't put me back in the dark!" I didn't care that my panic was evident in my voice. Anything to not be thrust back into that pit of despair.

Rough hands grabbed me. I flailed and kicked, cursed and screamed. Another soldier joined the first one to manhandle me back into the cage. I didn't care that their rough grips would leave bruises on my skin. I didn't care that one of the soldiers deliberately grabbed my breast when he had the opportunity. I only cared about the fact that they were pushing me back into the cell. They threw me inside and I instantly scrambled on all fours to get back out. That was the only thought in my mind. To get out. I was halfway back outside when one of the guards kicked me in the face. I heard my nose crunch an instant before the pain blinded me. My arms failed me and I collapsed to the ground, face first. My already broken nose struck the ground and I passed out in a flash of red-hot agony.

All was pain and darkness but the previous silence was replaced by a wet wheezing sound. It took me a while to understand that it was the breath whistling through my mangled nose. I fought the icy claws of dread scratching at the edge of my sanity and gingerly probed my face. Even the slightest touch of my fingertips on my swollen skin was agony. I had been punched before. I had been kicked. I had been cut. But I had never had my nose broken before and it was unlike any pain I had ever felt before. I had been taught that getting stabbed in the kidney was the most extreme pain a human could experience but if it was worse than this... I didn't even want to think about it. My sluggish mind turned another rotation and I realised that I should still have access to Blood Magic. The Bizhjerai bonds didn't suppress that since it did not really use any magical power as fuel. I closed my eyes and tried to focus, tried to find my centre so that I could look inward. My face throbbed and I was chilled through the bone. I was hungry, thirsty, and had a desperate need to empty my bladder. Focusing was impossible, would have been even if it hadn't been for the barely suppressed panic that roiled within my Soul.

"Focus, Mary! Relax, you dumb bitch!" I cursed at myself.

"Fuck!" I screamed when that elusive state of mind kept slipping away. Whenever I felt the trance-like state slip over me my broken nose would throb, or a violent shiver would wrack my body or a sudden fearful thought would blaze through my mind. It had been years since I had had any trouble entering Blood Magic mode. I had even done it in the midst of combat without a second thought. I'd done it with a shattered hand. But that pain had been like nothing to this. This one permeated my entire being. If I had just been able to enter that state for just a moment I could have used my Blood Magic to numb the pain. But apparently, that was not going to happen. I succumbed to the inevitable and rolled onto my side and curled into a shivering ball. Had I overplayed my hand? Was I already out of the game? It wasn't even endgame yet! But it was looking bleak indeed for me. If they hadn't already identified Alissa then it was only a matter of time before someone did. Once they knew that she was the Turncloak and that she was somehow affiliated with me... Even if I had been innocent I would probably be deemed guilty by association. The Skylands had never been one for due process. That was just something that existed in the old legends of the time before the Apocalypse. I would probably die here, and soon. The oath that I swore to my mothers that I would kill all Skylanders would remain unfulfilled. But perhaps I could take as many of them with me as possible. A grim grin stole across my lips. I still had the little crystals embedded in my breasts. If I could crush them then my favours would be called in. Kendall, Lukas and Raistlin would come. It would be far too late for me but the Groundlands wouldn't be able to hold against such a powerful onslaught. Or would it? If it hadn't been for Varl and his cadre of Construction Mages and the Prowler then I wouldn't have doubted for a moment. Especially not with the sabotaged defences I had rigged. But now? With a Skyship protecting the Groundlands and whatever improvements Varl would be able to raise in addition to fixing my sabotage... Perhaps it could hold. Lukas only commanded one ship and Raistlin had only promised me one of his own. It would depend on how many ships Kendall brought. He had mentioned rallying the whole Bizhjerai fleet but I knew for a fact that that was not a realistic expectation. I grimaced through the pain and tried to consider how many ships he could bring. Out of the thirteen ships in the fleet I knew that three were on a long range expedition to the Great Geyser above which the Skylands had once floated. Kendall had been considering a method of sealing a ley-line crossing that he might use to once and for all destroy the last traces of the Skylands. That geyser was an untapped source of magical power, one that raised the ambient magic field to dangerous levels. Now that the Skylands were gone and didn't absorb the magic then Wild Magic was likely to manifest in ever increasing frequency. They might even reach the shore given enough time. A further four ships were on constant patrol around the borders of Clan Territory. A number where on trading missions deeper inland. At the most there were ever three or four Skyships in the dry-docks of Dun Traelak and usually at least one of them was not sky-worthy. That meant that in all likelihood Kendall could only bring three Skyships, including the one that Lukas had promised me. Four Skyships, including Raistlin's contribution. Was that enough to defeat the Prowler? Without a doubt. Was it enough to deal with the Groundlands? Probably. But both together? In support of one another? It would be more even. Even if the Groundlands were defeated there would be heavy casualties among the attackers. Was my revenge worth the cost in lives? It was worth mine, no doubt. But did I have the right to demand that same sacrifice from others?

A part of me believed so. They had signed up as crew willingly to support the Bizhjerai and the other Clans through battle and trade. They had sworn to fight our enemies. The Groundlands may not be our enemies today but they would undoubtedly be in the future.

I screwed my eyes shut, unwilling to think about it. Then another thought struck me. Can I even call in my favours in my current condition?

I groped at my breasts, probing with my fingers until I found the hard lumps embedded within. I tentatively squeezed one of them between my fingers in a vain attempt to crush it. I was not strong enough to pulverise materialised magic crystal with my fingers. Even if I had had access to my Blood Magic I probably wouldn't. I pinched until I felt some tissue that lay between the crystal and the tips of my fingers give way with a squelching sensation but the pain that lanced through my chest was as nothing compared to the one that permeated my face. I had to get them out of me, then I might be able to use the heel of my boot to stomp them. But how would I get them out? Even if I managed to make a hole to reach the crystals I couldn't use telekinesis to extract them, not with the magical fetters on. I would have to make a hole large enough for my fingers to reach in and grab it. Could I endure that pain without numbing my flesh with Blood Magic? Maybe. First things first. I needed something to cut with. Theoretically I could probably use my fingernails to gradually scratch deeper and deeper into my flesh...

I had been relieved of my weapons, which included my dagger. I had nothing sharp. But then I remembered that my Angel armour had had its wings removed forcibly. I had felt the sharp edges with my own fingers.

I reached over my shoulder... Only to slap at bare skin. My heart sank. Varl had destroyed my armour. But perhaps there were some shards left, something that I could sharpen by using the walls of my cell as a grindstone. I patted myself down before ripping off what remained of my corset. Without seeing anything in the dark I let my cold fingers explore the tattered garment. Nothing. The only remaining shards that remained would not be long enough to cut all the way to the crystals embedded inside of me. I briefly considered using the plates of my greaves they it would be too large and unwieldy even if I managed to sharpen it. Without magic of any kind or tools there were simply no way for me to break off a suitable piece.

I slumped back into my corner, defeated. I pulled my knees up to my bare breasts, hugged my legs and rested my forehead on top of my knees. I didn't try to fight the tears any longer. I had lost. In my hubris I had taken on a task that was too great for me. And I would pay for it with my life. Not only my own, but Alissa's as well.

I slept fitfully, painfully, in the tight space. I was not certain if I slept, or when, for it was equally as dark and my racing thoughts were as terrifying as the nightmares. I couldn't separate the awake from the dream. All I knew is that I at times where screaming my throat raw, raging, begging, crying and pleading for them to let me out. My knuckles and fists ached and oozed blood that I could not see and I had lost more than one fingernail in my frantic scratching. They didn't feed me and my belly was gnawing with hunger. I had long since given up any concern for sleeping in my own filth and my cell and myself reeked with offal. When I heard a faint scratch on the outside of my prison I was almost dismissed it as another hallucination, but when I heard it again I jostled to attention. I crawled over to the corner where I had heard the sound.

"Hello?" I croaked hopefully, fearfully.

Then the side of the cell fell away and I was roughly dragged into the sunlight. I was blinded and my ears rang with a thunderous sound that I could not make sense of. But I was out in clean air and out of the cold. I drew the air deep into my lungs and smiled blissfully with my eyes closed against the glare.

I was suddenly aware of strong hands around my biceps and that I was being dragged across the ground. I forced my fraying mind into something that resembled cohesion and I prised my eyes open a sliver. I could make out rough shapes and colours in the harsh glare of the sun but focus was rapidly coming back to me. I was shoved roughly onto the ground and I cracked my chin. Only instinct had warned me enough to retract my tongue so that I didn't bite through it and instead my teeth clacked together painfully. I weakly pushed to my knees and stared around me, blinking. Faces. Hundreds of faces. I was back on the stage. A trial?

"You little bitch!" A familiar voice screeched at me. "We took you in after the Fall! And this is how you repay us? Betrayal?"

I forced my vision to clear enough to look at Aunt Alissa. Her face was a twisted mass of rage and hatred. I could barely recognise her. Her words lanced me through the heart. But she had known that I was here on a secret mission. Didn't she?

I had spoken to her several times before I was caught. I was sure of it. At least as sure as I could have been after the last few harrowing days. It must have been days, right?

"Give her some magic, Varl," a rough voice commanded. "She's barely cognisant and I want to get to the bottom of this."

Power washed over me and my Soul drank it in like a dry sponge finally allowed a trickle of water. My mind expanded, grew and stretched almost forgotten limbs. My thoughts quickened and my vision sharpened.

I looked at Alissa again. She really did give the impression that she hated me. Had she tried to rescue me and gotten caught? Or had something else happened?

Micah towered over me and glared at me with a furious scowl. "Why would Alissa Turncloak attempt to free you?"

"I..." I began but Alissa cut me off.

"I wasn't trying to free her, you buffoon! I was trying to kill her! She betrayed us. She betrayed me! After all that we did for you?"

Micah let her finish before he turned his stony gaze to me.

"Where did you learn magic? You certainly did not learn it from Mathias Varl."

"Please, Micah, allow me," Varl interjected smoothly. Alissa gave me a glare and when she caught me looking, she gave me a tiny shake of her head. Her two of her fingers curled into a sign in the nonverbal battle-language of Skylander mercenaries. Play along, it meant. She had tried to hide the sign from the crowd with her body and I dearly hoped that she had succeeded. I rallied my thoughts and prepared to fall in line with the lie Alissa had established.

Then Varl spoke and ruined it all. "I have perfected a spell recently that will allow us to know if they speak a lie," he announced smugly.

"That's impossible," Micah protested, seemingly forgetting that they had a crowd of hundreds of people.

"Not at all, old friend. It is not simple, but far from impossible. It's about intent, you see. I can tap into their intent and if it is to speak untruth then the spell will react accordingly."

Micah was silent for a long while before he nodded. "Very well, old friend. It can do no harm I suppose. Proceed."

Varl smiled and sketched a sarcastic bow towards his friend before he cast a complex spell on both me and Alissa. I could feel something latch onto my mind, but I couldn't tell what. Now how the hell would we be able to pull off any ruse that Alissa had concocted.

"Who taught you magic?" Micah asked me again.

My mind spun furiously but I couldn't see a way past Varl's spell. "The Bizhjerai," I finally responded.

The crowd let out a collective gasp and a loud murmur washed over me. Micah held up his hand for silence.

"Why would they teach you, a Skylander?"

"They took me in after my parents died," I tried. It wasn't a lie, not really, but it was not the entire truth. Anjali and Kyra had taken me in after my parents had died, but there were a severe time discrepancy that went unsaid. When nothing happened I let out a relieved little sigh and decided to elaborate. "After the war, many orphans were taken in and trained in magic."

Also technically the truth, though to my knowledge there hadn't been any Skylander orphans amongst them. Still Varl's spell didn't register a lie. He peered at his spellwork suspiciously though.

So, the spell would, if it actually worked as Varl claimed, detect an actual lie but partial or misleading truths seemed to slip by the net.

"Why did you come here?" Micah asked.

"I've always longed to join up with the rest of the surviving Skylanders," I said and stared into his eyes defiantly. What I had said was the absolute truth. I just hadn't voiced my reasons for wanting it.

"Even though you grew up with the enemy?"

"At the time I had little choice," I answered boldly. That was also true. I had only been a little girl back then and adults had taken the decision to move me from the Skylands. But I didn't have to say that given the choice then I would have done the same.

"Then why did you stay there?"

"I believed the Skylands to have been ruined. Would you have left a place that would clothe you, feed you and teach you how to fight and work magic?"

Apparently Micah had nothing to counter that with because he changed track entirely. "Why the matching tattoos?"

"It's a disguise, a temporary tattoo to hide the Bizhjerai Clan mark," I told him honestly.

"You where adopted into the Clan?" He said with obvious surprise.

"I was. I grew up in Dun Traelak."

"You traitorous bitch!" Alissa suddenly spat and with a gesture from Micah one of the guards struck her to silence her.

"Why did you hide this from us?"

I raised my arms to take in the entirety of the situation. "Wouldn't this have happened the moment someone recognised my Bizhjerai Rune? Wouldn't I be judged guilty by association?"

"I raised you!" Alissa suddenly screeched furiously. "I took you in after the war. Both Kendall and me. We raised you as our own and now you've joined our enemy? You sicken me, Mariana or Mary or whatever the fuck you call yourself here. I should have killed you after I found that poison in the nook in the wall," Alissa proclaimed loudly.

It took me a while to parse all of that and how she hadn't triggered the lie detection spell. Even though the Bizhjerai had already accepted me after the war, it was true that Kendall and Alissa had treated me as their own after the war after mom and mother had died. She had managed to obfuscate my name by referencing the unknown, the 'whatever the fuck I call myself'. If they had learned my true identity then they would probably have killed me on the spot. But I couldn't make sense of the rest, or that bit about the poison. Did I really sicken her?

"So let me summarise;" Micah began. "After Utopeia was destroyed you where lost and alone. Your family killed in the war," he gave me a glance and I nodded in confirmation. So far he was correct, though in other ways than he believed.

"Kendall and Alissa took you in, trained you and you were adopted into Clan Bizhjerai. Years later you heard about the Groundlands and you left your home to rejoin your people and use the teachings of our enemies to aid us."

Fuck! That last part was not true at all. I had come here to kill them all. Then a thought flashed through my mind. But I had come here to help them. At least initially. I wanted this place to attract more Skylanders before I destroyed them all. I focused on that angle with all of my power before I cleared my throat.

"Yes," I said loud and clear. I waited with bated breath for Varl's spell to signal that I had been lying.

But it didn't. I let out a relieved breath.

"It is a grand tale," Micah began with his back to me and facing the crowd.

"I find it hard to believe that you would turn on the woman that raised you so quickly to join with a group of strangers."

My heart sank. I hadn't managed to convince him.

"Though actions speak louder than words," Micah continued and I felt a flutter of hope. "Varl tells me that many of your creations were flawed, but he grudgingly admits that he could not prove that the flaws were deliberate and not due to incompetence. I suppose that one can not expect excellence from one that has been trained by savages that attempt to copy their betters."

Micah suddenly spun and pressed a crystal object into my palm. "Kill the Turncloak, the woman that raised you. Prove before us all where your loyalties lie," he cried and stepped back.

I stared down into my lap and the object he had given me. Mother's revolver. Slowly his words sank into my mind. He wanted me to kill Alissa. My Aunt. My friend. I looked up at her, panic making my thoughts jumbled.

She looked back at me with a mask of rage, but her eyes were strangely calm. Nobody that didn't know her would be able to see that. She gave me a tiny nod before she let her head slump forward so that hey blue hair fell down to hide her face from the sides. Only I could see her clearly. She winked at me and shot me a wan grin. I took a deep breath and struggled to my feet. I checked the small gap between the cylinder and the stock of my revolver. Only one shot. I walked over to Alissa and stood above her. Even though she was kneeling I was not that much taller than her.

"What do I do?" I whispered so softly that only she could hear me.

"Do it. Kill me, regain their trust and fulfil your mission", she whispered back just as softly.

My heart broke. A hundred thoughts flashed through my mind. I considered turning around and shooting Micah. I considered shooting Varl. Shooting the Guard behind Alissa and fight tooth and nail while screaming for her to flee, spending my life to save hers. I considered putting the barrel to my own head and pulling the trigger. But all of those actions would lead to my death, and then shortly thereafter Alissa's too. There were no way out of here alive for her. Not now that they knew her identity and had her at her mercy.

"Silence, Turncloak," Micah snapped and signalled for the guard to hit her again.

The crunch of the steel gauntlet against the side of her head snapped something inside of me. My mind went completely still, my face entirely blank. I blinked once and a single tear from each eye rolled down my cheeks.

I raised the revolver and aimed it at my Aunts face. She looked into my eyes above the barrel, calmly.

I adjusted my sights a little higher to aim at the point between her eyebrows. The muzzle wobbled ever so slightly, the only outwards tell that my calm was but a thin veneer.

My thumb cocked the hammer and my finger tightened on the trigger.

She was already dead. There were no way out of this alive for her. If I didn't do this, then I would also be dead soon. Better that she die by the hand of someone that loves her. That would make sure that she died cleanly and painlessly. Someone who would make sure that all of these bastards would soon follow her to whatever afterlife may exist.

I saw only pride in her eyes. Pride and grim acceptance of her fate. She gave me a tiny nod, urging me on before closing her eyes and smiling sadly toward the sun.

The sound of that gunshot and the following thud of finality would haunt my nightmares for the rest of my life.


---

## Chapter 21 — CHAPTER TWENTY-ONE Red flare {#chapter-21}

CHAPTER TWENTY-ONE

Red flare

The gun dropped from my nerveless fingers but I managed to stay upright, swaying. I couldn't tear my eyes from Aunt Alissa's crumpled form or the spray of brain, bone, blood and hair that was spread out behind her. Her hair had fallen to cover the fact that the back of her head was mostly missing and if it hadn't been for the large bullet hole in her forehead she could have been mistaken for sleeping peacefully. But then her body was wracked with death throes and her face grimaced and twitched before finally slackening unnaturally. I let out a strangled sob, quickly subdued even though it felt that I would burst apart by doing so.

Then the crowd exploded in cheers and jubilation that the hated Turncloak was finally dead. Only one of the architects of the war still lived in their minds. Kendall. Mom was dead. Mother was dead. Grandfather as well. Even Iolan, the fool chief of Clan Ithlak that had started the war was dead, his heart ripped out of his chest by mother after mom's cremation. Only Kendall survived. My mother's memories of Alissa flashed through my mind. How they had shared the wonder of the new world they had found themselves in after embarking on the First Expedition. How they had shared hopeful banter and camaraderie, even flirted with one another while they explored the Elweyr forest. Then they had fought that bear and been separated only to be reunited much later in Dun Traelak. Alissa had always been there working in the shadows. She had supported mother in everything and had fought valiantly in the war. She had taken me under her wing when my mothers had been slain. I couldn't reconcile that Alissa with the dead flesh in front of me. Dead because of me. Because of my thirst for vengeance.

"Take her back to her cell," Micah ordered.

The stab of panic I felt at the prospect of being returned to that dreadful box barely manage to pierce the thick blanket of darkness that enveloped my heart. I had killed Alissa. I couldn't take my eyes away from her even as they dragged me away. When I was finally out of sight of her all of my remaining strength left me and I hung limp between the two men. I didn't even protest when they heaved me into the box and sealed me inside. I had killed Alissa. Slowly the tears started to fall while I stared into the darkness. Over and over again I saw the hole appear in her forehead and her hair lifting as if in a stiff breeze before it was hurled backward. Then along with the hair came the gush of mangled brain and the sick soft splatter mixed with the hard clatter of bone on crystal floor. And then that final series of thuds. First her elbow striking the floor, then her shoulder and finally what remained of her skull. The slow trickle of tears became a torrent and before long I was screaming my pain and grief and regret into my horrible box. I doubted anyone outside could hear me, and I didn't care if they could. I had killed Alissa. My Aunt. The fourth in the long line of women that had been my mothers. The last mother I would ever have. I don't know how long I cried and howled, but no matter how long my pain did not abate. I might have passed out at some point, but if that was the case then I woke up crying.

Eventually I had no more tears to cry and I could not sob without my aching belly muscles forcing a dry heave. My mind stilled from the raging torrent of grief into a still pond of despair. My mind went blank for I know not how long. When the box opened I didn't even turn my head to the light. I just stared straight ahead, eyes blinded. Not even the light could evoke any tears.

"Is she sleeping?" A soft voice asked from outside.

"I don't think so," another answered. "She's sitting up. Mary?"

I didn't react. Couldn't. The light was blocked when a large form crawled into the box beside me.

Strong arms looped around my shoulders and beneath my legs and gently pulled me out of my prison. It was twilight, but I could see quite clearly through the murk. It was Zandor, and next to him stood Valia.

"Mary?" Valia asked me, wringing her hands anxiously.

"Why isn't she responding?" Valia asked Zandor, concern tinging her voice heavily.

"She's in shock, or she's exhausted. You would be too if you'd been through what she's been. Let's get her home."

Without waiting for our lover Zandor carried me away. I didn't pay attention. Nothing mattered any more. Nothing but killing all of these people.

Suddenly we were back in my workshop and Zandor put me down on my feet. Somehow I remained standing, staring at nothing.

Valia started working on my belt buckle and eventually had my pants down by my ankles.

"Mary, help me out here," she pleaded.

Numbly I lifted first one leg and then the other. Valia pushed me down on a stool and then shot Zandor a fearful glance.

The big man just shrugged and handed Valia a sponge. Together they cleaned me off as best as they could before Zandor stood behind me with a pail of water and Valia washed my hair in it.

Valia stared at my hair for a long while until I felt the water start trickling out of it as if it had become hydrophobic. I had seen mom use a similar spell to dry her hair many a time.

Zandor carried me to our bed and tucked me in before kissing me on the forehead.

"I'll be right back. Try to rest, love," he whispered hoarsely before retreating to have a word with Valia.

They only spoke briefly before stripping down and sliding beneath the covers on either side of me. Zandor made sure that I was snuggled up to his chest with his strong arms embracing me and Valia scooted up along my front and pressed my face into her bosom. Safe between my two lovers, the tears returned. I cried quietly while Valia stroked my hair and whispered comforting nothings into my ear and Zandor gently rubbed my belly with his thick fingers.

Eventually I managed to fall into a sleep plagued by nightmares in which everyone I loved died, over and over again.

It had taken me days to recover enough sense to even remotely resemble a human being once again. The grief hadn't lessened, my disgust with myself was as sharp as it had been the moment I had squeezed that trigger. But that storm of emotions had focused into a single goal. To kill all Skylanders. Unfortunately they had made that rather difficult. Apparently my killing of Alissa had convinced enough of the Skylanders that they should let me live. I was too useful to kill outright even after Varl had arrived with his cadre of skilled Construction Mages. They were particularly interested in my spell to open ley line crossings and create the artificial ley line system. Only the Bizhjerai knew how to do that and they had not been able to figure it out over the last decade. I believed firmly that the moment they had learned to do it themselves then I would die in some 'accident' and not many people would bother to look into it. Varl and his Mages had inspected all of my creations and corrected any and all of the flaws that I had included that they could find. I didn't yet know the extent of my sabotage that had been counter-sabotaged but it didn't matter. I had to figure out some other way to disable the Groundlands when the time came. I just didn't know how. So I had spent any time I could spare in the last two weeks on the meta-magic spell that would find new spells. The current iteration of the spell could in theory figure out the smallest of light spells, though it hadn't produced any actual output yet. Even if it did then it would be less than useless in and of itself. It was just a proof of concept. But if it did work, then perhaps I could use it to figure out a spell that could disable to Skylands. Perhaps a magical disease that would spread through the settlement and kill everyone, or maybe a contagious curse. I just didn't know what would be possible, but it would most likely surprise me. So I let it work through the vast array of potential spells while I threw myself into my task of ensuring the destruction of the Groundlands. I needed the distraction, only the planning of destruction could keep my grief at bay. I couldn't express it openly, I was supposed to have hated Alissa and the Bizhjerai, and that made it so much worse.

Varl's arrival had had a silver lining though. In his travels across the lands in search for us he had broadcasted a message to all Skylanders to gather and wait for him to return to fetch them. His force of Construction Mages had in short order retrofitted the Skyship to be a passenger carrier. There were several locations throughout the Cities that would serve as gathering posts for the Skylanders that had scattered to the winds after the Fall and now that the Groundlands had been found then the Prowler would ferry them back here. Thousands more would arrive in short order. There were already almost seven thousand Skylanders in the Groundlands, there couldn't be that many more out there. Perhaps the same number again. I had originally believed that it would take almost a year before I could bring my plan into fruition but with the aid of the Prowler then it might be as short as a few weeks. The Prowler had been gone a couple of days already. Varl and a great number of his Mages had remained behind though and one of them was always with me both to keep an eye on me since Varl did not trust me and also to try to prise my secrets from me. So far I had been able to teach them only disjointed parts of the spells that would not allow them to cast the spell themselves. But soon I would not be able to dissemble any longer. I would have to actually teach them the valuable things.

In preparation for the sudden influx of hundreds if not thousands of additional Skylanders I had been sent out to open more ley line crossings. Of course that insufferable Mage of Varl's had followed along. Micah had switched out my crew and I didn't know any of them. He had known that Valia and Zandor was too close to me and he no longer trusted them fully either. I had managed to dodge my chaperone for this particular ley line crossing since it was way up in the mountain and he did not have an Angel armour of his own. Not yet. I had been forced to give up my choker for a few days so that Varl's people could set up manufactorium spells for the Angel pattern armour. The mountain split apart before me and power washed over me.

Mother cast a single glance at my face before she wrapped me into her embrace. "What has happened?" She whispered into my ear.

I couldn't keep the tears back. I don't know how long I cried into her spectral shoulder, but eventually I managed to draw enough breath to speak.

I didn't want to tell her. I feared that she would hate me for killing her friend. But she deserved to know. She was my mother.

"I killed Alissa," I whimpered and I felt mother go stiff and solid beneath my hands. Then she slowly softened and her crystal hand kept stroking at my hair.

"Tell me what happened," she ordered me gently.

So I did. I tried to retell as many details as I could but many where lost in my incoherent sobs. When I finally finished mother was silent for a long while. I pulled back and risked to look up into her face. Glowing tears where flowing freely from her shining eyes.

"You did the right thing, my daughter," mother finally whispered. "Though it pains me to know that Alissa is dead, I still believe that you did the right thing."

"You do?" I whimpered.

"I do," she nodded, "I think that your analysis of the situation was correct. Alissa's life was over the moment she got caught trying to break you free. You gave her a quick death."

"But she died because of me!"

"No, daughter. She died because of her choice to help you. You may have been the one to pull the trigger, but it was the Skylanders that killed her," mother said with finality. "And soon, we will kill them all. Tell me about your plan now that Varl is down there?"

"I don't have a plan," I admitted. "With my sabotage undone, I don't know how to ensure that the fleet will crush the Groundlands. I don't even know if it can if the Prowler is here as support."

"There are other ways to weaken the defences than the perfect dismantling of the defences."

"There is?" I asked, confused.

Despite her apparent grief mother chuckled.

"You were always such a perfectionist, just like you mom. You don't have to disable all of the guns in the Groundlands. For instance, the field artillery and small arms, even armour, will be useless against the Skyships. Focus on the defences that will be an actual obstacle for the fleet. Anti air guns. Shields. The Prowler. Boarding parties."

"How do I stop that?" I truly had no clue how to achieve that.

Mother shrugged. "Do what I always did. When in doubt, blow shit up."

"Blow shit up?" I echoed incredulously.

"Yes. Whatever there are that supports these defencive functions, destroy them. Destroy them in a manner and timing that does not allow the Groundlands to adjust to the sudden change in defencive capability. You don't need to remove all guns and smash the walls down to let the fleet in. Just open the door for them and let them do what they do best."

I nodded, still not sure what to do but at least her advice made some sense.

Mother reached out with her hand and caressed my cheek. "Don't think about Alissa any more, daughter. She made her choice and she paid with her life for it. Just like we all will one day. Focus on your mission, there will be time for grief later."

I nodded and blinked the stubborn tears out of my eyes. "I will, mother."

"Now, I will return to the dagger. I wish to keep a healthy reserve of power in the gem so that I can appear if you need me."

I hugged her hard, and then she was gone. I waited by the budding focusing tower, waiting for my swollen and puffy eyes to return to something that resembled normalcy and tried desperately to calm my swirling emotions.

Mother had given me a fresh sense of direction, even though I was not yet certain what that direction was. But I needed to figure out a way to cripple the Groundlands defences against Skyships.

I returned to the other crossing where my chaperone was waiting and I cast the spell as quickly as I could so that he would have as little time as possible to figure it out. He scowled at me but I just shrugged. I was sure that he was going to report my reluctant cooperation back to Varl but I still believed that the only thing that kept me alive was that they did not know how to open ley line crossings.

Thankfully there had been a prototype Skymmer in the holds of the Prowler that Varl and his apprentices had attempted to reverse engineer after having spied on the Bizhjerai. I had reluctantly helped them perfect the design and it was now fully operational. The Skymmers where a creation of Kendall's that used a sort of telekinesis spell to remain airborne and to provide thrust. There where still some kinks in the spell which reduced the velocity proportional to the altitude. In laymen terms, the higher you flew the slower you flew. At a certain altitude it was completely stationary. For some bizarre reason that neither Kendall not I could figure out this absolute maximum altitude was not in relation to the surface below but seemed somehow linked to the altitude of the ocean. That's why we hadn't been able to take the Skymmer to the ley line crossing where I had had my most recent conversation with mother. It was simply too high above the ocean. We returned to the Groundlands well before sundown and I was surprised indeed to see the Prowler taking off. A huge crowd of lost looking people stood on the plains to the north of the Skylands. It would appear as if the first batch of newcomers had arrived. By my estimation it was almost a thousand people down there. The Prowler must have been packed from floor to ceiling to fit that many people aboard. My chaperone set the Skymmer down near my workshop and I left him behind without a word and entered my abode. Neither Zandor nor Valia was there and I took the opportunity to think about my next course of action. After a few minutes of a complete lack of ideas I decided to do what mother had suggested.

"When in doubt, blow shit up," I muttered to myself.

The artificial ley line that supplied my workshop with magical power provided more than enough power for my meta-magic spell and I decided that I should use that surplus power for something useful.

Casting a spell that would create a power core was relatively simple. Embedding a simple explosive spell into it was even easier. Now I only had to hide them away from Valia and Zandor. Ever since they had been practising with their spells they had become more curious about what I was doing. Then I realised that I didn't need to hide them. I could just put them on my workbench and if they asked I could just spout some advanced sounding bullshit until they lost interest. And that was exactly what I did when Valia turned up and asked me what I was doing. The hopeful expression on her face when she saw me being productive almost broke my heart. I had been a mess lately and her worry had been palpable. She interrupted my harangue of hogwash with her lips, but my heart was not into it.

"I'm sorry," I apologised when she pulled back from me. "I'm just..."

"Hush," she said and placed her finger gently across my lips. "I understand. That trial was..." She shuddered instead of finishing. "I love you, Mary. Whatever you need. Just let me know."

I nodded and couldn't help but smile at my beautiful lover. She deserved so much more than me. I stood up and went up on my tippy toes to kiss her. "Soon," I promised her.

"I know," she murmured into my hair while she crushed me to her chest.

Before I could respond, the door was flung open and Zandor burst into the room.

"The flare," he gasped out between choking breaths. "It's red!"

"Shit!" I cursed and dashed out of our home to look for myself. True enough, the flare was high in the sky and burned an angry red. That meant that the first focusing tower that we had erected had somehow become compromised. There where hostiles in the area that the automated defences could not deal with.

"Should we go? Or should we let it go?" Valia asked.

The focusing tower wasn't critical any more. The loss of that one would not be a terrible blow. But it would set us back. The Groundlands were a serious manufactorium at that point. Even though we had two more focusing towers on the rise we were already pushed to the limit. The loss of that tower would cost us a lot of progress until it was replaced, and after that it would take us a long time to catch up. It would also force me to open more crossings and I doubted that I could keep Varl's Mages from learning the spell again.

"We go," I decided. "It's probably just a jammed barrel."

"Good," Zandor agreed, "Felaan and Wylah are preparing the bikes. They should be ready when we get there," he finished and set off at a run.

His longer legs left me and Valia in the dust and he and the two stoic warriors were already astride their bikes when we shorter girls caught up to them.

We rode through the night with that angry red glare in our faces. Perhaps we should have stopped to rest at some point, but we didn't. If we had then maybe our mission would have been more successful than it was.

I remember that we decided to ditch the bikes a way from the tower and approach on foot. While the engines were not that loud, the sound of the tires cracking branches and twigs were. We advanced as a troop, Wylah and I bringing up the rear with our rifles while Zandor took point with his massive halberd. Valia and Felaan brought up the flanks. We approached the tower and I peered through the gloom of the sunrise. Hundreds of savages littered the ground in various stages of decay. Many of the corpses appeared fresh. Had they simply thrown enough bodies at the turrets to exhaust the ammunition caches? Maybe, but the manufactorium spells should have had enough time to replenish the cache while we rode here but the red flare had been refreshed many times during our journey. I realised that we hadn't even paused long enough to check in with Micah. Perhaps he was sending an army up here already.

"Let me switch off the turrets and have a closer look," I whispered to my friends. I fished the token out of my satchel and quickly deactivated the defences of the tower. My Angel armour was already manifested and with a sharp shriek of the engines it lifted me into the air. My friends wisely remained concealed and out of range of the turrets. If the savages arrived then I could reactivate the turrets and hide outside of their field of vision, perhaps on top of the tower, and let the automated defences deal with them. I checked the caches and found that they were all empty. That was strange. I checked the manufactorium spell and found the issue. It had degraded from continuous use and it had fallen apart. I cursed. It would take a long while for me to repair it and even longer for it to replenish the ammunition caches. It would probably be easier for me to rip it all apart and build a new one from scratch. I angled my wings to spin myself around to return to my friends so that I could appraise them of the situation. That's when I saw them. Hundreds of savages had managed to sneak up behind my friends in the forest. It was a trap. They had triggered the red flare and then circled around whatever relief force that may arrive. They were craftier than their appearance foretold. I spun around and gave the turrets a final glance. Just as I thought. No chance of them helping us out. I soared back to my friends and landed heavily.

"It's a trap, there's hundreds of them behind us. We have to move, now!" I told them. I didn't bother keeping my voice down. They would have seen me clearly descending from the tower.

To their credit, my friends didn't hesitate a moment and they started running. I soared into the sky to act as scout. The horde had indeed figured out what was going on and with a collective howl of fury they burst into a charge to catch up with my friends. I unslung my rifle and aimed down the crystal sights. There was no way that I would be able to snipe while flying but I didn't really need to be accurate. I thumbed a firestorm spell into the shot in the breech and I lined it up in the general direction of the thickest congregation of savages. I squeezed the trigger. The shot erupted in a large storm of shrapnel and fire. Too late I remembered that these creatures drank magic. The fire winked out when the power that fuelled it was consumed but to my surprise there were several mangled bodies on the ground, a few of them were still writhing in pain. There where also a substantial amount of fire that remained. Curious. It would appear that even though the magic that caused the fire was gone, whatever had been damaged or accelerated by magic retained those effects. That would be useful indeed in any future altercations with this people. If I survived to report this to the Groundlands, that is. I quickly squeezed off the rest of my shots, all of them firestorms. This time I made sure to not aim directly at the savages but instead I shot at the ground before their charge. Without their strange magic-drinking aura the shot ravaged the land and a wall of flame kept them at bay. I zipped back to my friends and landed beside them running.

"I've bought us some time but it won't last long. There might be more of them."

"What's the plan?" Zandor asked, breathing heavily.

The big man was strong and almost unstoppable in melee combat, but stamina was not his strength.

"Run, hide in the slopes of the mountain," Felaan answered from across Zandor. The dark ranger didn't even seem out of breath despite the rapid pace.

"I'll scout ahead," I gasped, already winded. With relief I shot into the sky and blasted toward the cliff wall. Perhaps there were some cave there, something with a choke point that we could defend if we couldn't hide from the horde. I reloaded my rifle as I flew, slotting the magazine with the standard kinetic rounds into the weapon and racking the bolt. A glint of light from ahead and below me drew my attention and I flew across it. It was a campfire with what appeared to be a spitted animal resting above the flames. The smell was wonderful. Beside the flame sat a female savage, slowly turning the spit, and looked up at me with glowing violet eyes. But what really drew my gaze was the opening in the cliff wall behind her. It was, indeed, a cave. Perhaps something that we could hide inside? I twisted in the air until I was soaring ass-first through the morning sky and I gunned the main engine to reduce my velocity. I swiftly started falling but my ethereal wings guided me down. I touched down on an outcropping far from the female. She had stood up and was shading her eyes with her hand while she peered in my direction. I lined up my crystal sights and shot her in the heart. Her large chest cavity appeared to turn inside out, gore spraying across the cliff wall behind her. The report of my gun echoed loudly between the canyon walls and I waited with bated breath for several seconds in case another savage would appear. It proved to be wise of me for waiting for two males burst out of the cave. One of them howled and fell to his knees beside the female, pulling her into his lap. The other one was scanning the horizon for threats. I put him down first with a shot to the temple. The other one bounced to his feet only to topple with a ripped apart lung from my next shot. He screamed in agony but he could not do much more with only one lung. I waited for more savages to appear but when several heartbeats had passed I felt the need to return to my friends. I leaped to the skies and quickly found them not too far away. It appeared as if they were running toward the sound of gunfire. Clever. The savages didn't have guns. The source of any such noise could only have been me. I dropped down in front of them.

"Caves. Over here," I said and took the lead. The only way I could stay ahead of my taller friends was if I used my Angel armour to give me a boost to lengthen my stride. Unfortunately that would give away our position since the screech of the engines were far from subtle. When we drew closer to the caves I stopped doing that and we had to slow down so that we could all stay together. By that point even Wylah and Felaan were breathing heavily. I cautiously led our little group into the caves, my revolver in my right hand and a light spell in my left. The cave was narrow, only broad enough for two men to walk shoulder to shoulder. Zandor was large enough to block it almost by himself. The cave vanished into darkness further into the mountains.

"Do we keep going or do we try to hold here?" I asked my friends.

"I don't fancy running deep into an unknown cave system with howling savages at my back," Zandor grumbled.

"I agree," Felaan mused. "At least here we have a choke point."

"But there's hundreds of them!" Valia protested.

"We've got guns," Wylah countered. "And magic. We can keep them at bay until the cavalry arrives."

"Cavalry?" I asked.

"Micah is bound to send people up here in response to the flare. They can't be that far behind us."

"It took us nearly two days to get here on foot," I countered.

Wylah frowned. Obviously she hadn't considered the fact that we were the only ones with bikes.

"So, what do you say? The unknown of the caves or the known at the entrance?" Zandor finally asked.

"Caves," I said instantly, quickly supported by Valia.

"Entrance," Felaan stated calmly and Wylah naturally fell in with him.

We all turned impatient expressions to Zandor.

For a moment he seemed torn, and then with a reluctant glance into the darkness of the caves he finally answered. "Caves."

We didn't waste any more time after that and we ran into the unknown darkness.


---

## Chapter 22 — CHAPTER TWENTY-TWO Savages {#chapter-22}

CHAPTER TWENTY-TWO

Savages

Shortly thereafter we reached a fork in the cave. Zandor cast a light spell to the right and Valia to the left. Just more caves in either direction, slightly sloping upward.

"Which way?" I gasped out between heaving breaths.

"Any, we've got company!" Felaan shouted over his shoulder while Wylah raised her rifle.

The sound of heavy footsteps and feral growls echoed up the cave we had just travelled through. I fell back to stand next to Wylah and I raised my rifle.

We had a rather long stretch of cave to fire down. Felaan stood next to me, a single shot pistol in his left and a revolver in his right.

"One volley to make them hesitate," Wylah snapped.

I didn't have time to answer for the savages burst into view. I took a bead on the one in the lead and blew her brains out. Wylah felled the one immediately behind her and Felaan unloaded both of his weapons. I saw one of his shots ping into the side of the cave, probably the smoothbore pistol, and one of the savages went down with a ruptured thigh. The other savages slowed down and crouched low in response, their shining eyes narrowed.

"Go!" Felaan snapped.

As one, the three of us spun on our heels and sprinted toward Valia and Zandor. They both ran to the right and the rest of us followed.

I feared that whatever we did we would soon be dead. The savages were larger than us and their longer legs could propel them faster than we could run. We couldn't outrun them.

"How are you holding up?" Felaan asked me with concern.

"Can't run much longer at this pace," I gasped out.

As the least fit member of our group, and the heaviest armoured, I was slowing us down. I was more of a scholar than a warrior, dammit!

"Use your armour," Wylah snapped before sprinting past me to scout ahead.

I leaned forward and did as I was told and I fired the main engine in short bursts to make me leap across the floor instead of sprinting.

Slowly I managed to regain my breath and then we suddenly burst into a large chamber. My light spell only illuminated a few yards in either direction and we skidded to a halt. Zandor caught my careening form and swung me around to bleed off my momentum. He finally set me down on my feet and I leaned against his thick frame for a moment.

"What now?" Valia asked with a tinge of panic in her voice. Despite her apparent distress her fingers clutched her sword and revolver with resolve.

"If this is a dead end, we are fucked," Felaan announced calmly.

"We can't risk it," Wylah agreed. "We gotta hold them in the choke until we can find a way out. If they catch us in here then we are done for."

Zandor nodded, hefted his halberd and positioned himself in the mouth of the opening, almost blocking it by himself. Wylah fell in by his shoulder, aiming her rifle along his side. I unslung my rifle and tossed it to Felaan who caught it smoothly and emulated his girlfriend on the other side of Zandor. Valia sheathed her sword and cast a bright light spell down the tunnel, illuminating a far stretch of it so that the others could shoot. I turned my back to them, spread my wings and slowly raised myself toward the roof. I cradled my hands before me, creating a light spell and scaling it up while filling it with power. When I was about twice of my height from the cave floor I cast it to hover above my head. The chamber was suddenly bathed in weak light and I gasped in surprise. The walls where lined in a sort of wooden walkway, and everywhere I looked rocks the size of heads had been lovingly placed into carved nests packed with wood shavings.

"What the fuck?" I murmured to myself. I shook my head and pushed the bizarre art installation from my mind. Exits. That's what I was looking for. Exits.

I could not find any, but that did not mean that there where none. The wooden walkways obscured my vision from my current vantage. I carefully looped around the cave wall, inspecting each walkway in an attempt to find an exit.

"Contact!" Wylah called out and gunfire instantly followed. Distorted howls of pain and fury echoed out of the tunnel along with what sounded eerily like guttural words spoken in a language not meant for human throats. I increased my speed as much as I dared.

"Here they come!" I heard Zandor yell and then I could hear him grunt with exertion. I cast a worried look over my shoulder and saw that he was leaning into the haft of his halberd, his whole body straining as he fought to hold the horde back.

All around him Felaan and Wylah and Valia where firing their weapons. With a heave Zandor pushed his halberd and then quickly retracted it before lashing out again. He was primarily using the long spike as a spear to hold the savages back.

"Reloading," Valia shouted and bowed her head to focus on her task.

"Hold them," Wylah shouted before she stepped back to reload. Felaan had also expended his shots but instead of reloading he lowered my rifle in his left hand and he drew his revolver with his right to keep the pressure up.

Remembering my task, I turned my attention from my friends in search of an exit. The gunshots and shouts from down below haunted me and I flew ever quicker. A single mistake now would send me careening headfirst into the wall.

There! What was that? A darker area had briefly revealed itself. I swung around and reversed my direction, coming to a hover in front of an opening in the wall. I flung a light spell into the hole. It was another tunnel. How far it led, I did not know. It might even be a dead end. Could we risk it? I could always fly into the narrow tunnel to scout it out a bit. It would be risky, a single clip of my wings would bring me tumbling down to possibly break my neck. I was about to do it when I heard a shout for below. I spun around and looked down. Zandor was being pushed out of the tunnel mouth and Felaan where thrusting with his sword at something I could not see. He must have exhausted the shots in my rifle. Wylah where furiously reloading and Valia where using her magic to hurl loose rocks over Zandor's head. They were being pushed back. I cut my engines and dropped. I used my ethereal wings to guide my fall and with a sharp snap I opened them up. I aimed my feet at the savage gripping the haft of Zandor's halberd beneath the head. My teeth jarred when my boots made impact but my sudden appearance had its desired effect. The savage grunted and its grip slipped. With a sudden lurch Zandor stepped forward and rammed the long spike into the savages heart. I twisted in the air and used short flares of my wings to land on my feet, revolver already in hand. I used my left hand to fan the hammer and fired six shots from the hip. Three savages fell to the cave floor, dying. Zandor leaped back into his position.

"There's an opening way up there," I shouted to be heard over the din.

"A way out?" Felaan asked while reloading his revolver a single shot at a time.

"Dunno, but its gotta be better than this?"

"Can't disengage," Zandor grunted and thrust with his halberd. "They'll swarm us the moment we leave the opening."

"On my mark!" Valia cried and I saw her fumble in her satchel.

She withdrew a fist-sized iron sphere. A black powder grenade! She frowned at the fuse, the tip of her small tongue poking out the corner of her mouth while she focused. Her hand cradled the fuse. I sensed a touch of magic and light erupted from between her fingers with an angry hiss.

"Fire in the hole," she yelled and rolled the grenade between Zandor's legs to glide into the tunnel mouth.

I waited with bated breath.

"Mark!" Valia screamed and leaped to the side. Wylah had already fallen back a ways up the walkway and Felaan and Zandor smoothly retreated to the side of the cave mouth to get away from the blast radius. I fired my engines and rose upward. Felaan tossed me my rifle and I caught it, my hand already digging in my satchel for a fresh magazine of kinetic rounds.

A savage warily stepped out of the tunnel mouth, and then the grenade exploded. The thunder of the blast was deafening in the enclosed space and I felt my ears pop. Smoke and fire burst out of the tunnel mouth and the savage was thrown on his face. I flew backwards to land lightly beside Wylah and I quickly reloaded my rifle before falling to a knee next to her, already aiming at the cloud of smoke. Felaan, Zandor and Valia were already sprinting toward the base of the ramp and Valia only paused to quickly stab the weakly struggling savage twice in the back. I watched her anxiously with one eye as she ran up the ramp and I winced in fear when her foot caught on one of the rocks and she fell.

"Fuck!" She grunted and quickly stood up. But she didn't keep running. My attention was taken by movement in the cave mouth and I squeezed the trigger at the same time as Wylah did. My shot struck the savage in the chest, hers in the head.

"Conserve ammo," Wylah hissed at me. "I'll shoot first. If there's more than one target, you take the one on the right. Shoot while the other reload."

"Got it," I responded and spared a glance at Valia. She was kneeling next to the rock she had tripped over.

"Valia, get a fucking move on," Zandor roared at her.

Valia flinched and then she was moving again. "They're not rocks," she yelled. "It's eggs!"

"What?" Zandor asked eloquently.

A savage burst out of the tunnel with a challenging roar and Wylah quickly put him down with a clean headshot.

"Savage eggs?" Felaan called back.

"I think so, it kinda looked like a human child in there," she responded and shuddered.

Another savage emerged, a female this time, quickly followed by a particularly large male. The male raised a javelin to throw at us and I quickly shot him in the chest and he dropped beside the suddenly headless female. More savages emerged, faster than Wylah and I could drop them.

"Keep going," I screamed at the others. "I will stay and provide cover. I can retreat with my armour!"

Thankfully they saw the sense in my words and sprinted up the ramp. I kept firing until the firing pin dropped on an empty chamber. They were pouring up the ramp by then and I did as Felaan had done earlier and held my rifle in my left hand and fired my revolver with my right. I didn't even bother trying to use magic against them. I fired my last shot into the chest of the female in the lead but a male merely leaped her falling body as if it was nothing. He raised his huge club to smash me into nothing. I spread my left wing and fired all of the jets on that side. I was wrenched off the ramp and into the open atrium where I used my other wing to stop my mad tumble before I fired the main engine to fly up to my friends. Wylah was laying down flanking fire from across the cave.

"Can't you do any magic to stop them?" She called out to me as I approached.

"No, they drink my power," I responded and landed beside her and started reloading my weapons.

"Shit," she grunted and squeezed off a shot. She realigned her aim and fired, but the pin struck an empty chamber. "Fuck! I'm dry. Let's go!"

She didn't wait for a response, instead she just ran.

I glanced after her and then decided to do something foolish. I spread my awareness out through my magesense in search of any loose objects nearby. I was inspired by Valia who had throw rocks at the savages. Anything to slow them down. But there where of course no rocks on the ramp. Only the eggs. The savages where getting close but I still had some time.

"Mary! Fucking run!" Valia screamed from above.

Telekinetic spells exploded out of me, latching on to any egg I could reach. My mind spun with the effort of controlling that many spells at once and I felt my body collapse onto the ramp. With a mighty flex of my mind dozens of eggs rose into the air. The savages skidded to a stop, all of them staring at the floating eggs. Then I hurled the eggs at them. The relief was palpable when the spells winked out one by one and I blinked my drying eyes.

Without rising I engaged all of the jets on my armour and I was tossed once more into open air. I was still a bit unsteady after my exertion and it took a lot of focus and effort to level out my flight but eventually I could rejoin my friends. I looked over my shoulder and saw a whole lot of the savages gently replace the eggs that they had caught. Several others were kneeling next to the crushed shells that remained of the rest, keening softly. But there were a lot of them that kept chasing us. We were almost at the top and with a cry of relief we burst into the tunnel. Strangely enough, Zandor spun by the entrance and took up a guarding position.

"Zandor?" I called out to him.

"Those where eggs," he said with determination filling his voice. "Hundreds of them. We can't let them hatch. They'll come for the Groundlands eventually, I can feel it."

"There's too many of them," Wylah snapped. "We have to report back, return with more soldiers."

"You go," he shouted over his shoulder to be heard over the din of howling savages that approached. "I'll stay and smash as many as I can."

"It's suicide!" I protested.

"Perhaps," he said calmly.

"Fool!" I cursed him and to my own great surprise I took up position behind him and peered around his waist.

"Mary, go!" Zandor ordered me angrily. "I'll hold them off, you heard Wylah! Bring the rest of the warriors!"

"I'm not leaving you," I hissed at him.

"Neither am I," Valia declared from his other side.

"No one is getting left behind," Felaan said calmly and cocked the hammer of his revolver.

"You don't happen to have another of those grenades, Valia?" Wylah asked and slammed what had to have been her last magazine into her rifle.

"Unfortunately not," the smaller woman replied with a grim grin.

"Here they come!" Zandor bellowed and smoothly skewered the first savage that rounded the corner and pushed her off the ramp to fall to her death below.

I was thinking furiously. I still had plenty of magical power left despite my stunt with the eggs. That hadn't drained me severely of power, it had just been difficult to cast that many spells so quickly. But I couldn't use my magic against these creatures, they'd just drink it up.

Zandor's halberd lanced into a savages eye and got stuck in the cranium. He cursed and tried to retract his weapon but the corpse locked it down. Another savage pounced on him. Valia flung out her hand and cast a tongue of flame at the savage. The flames winked out before they could do more than singe the skin of the savages face but the creature still flinched back. That distraction was all that Zandor needed and he crunched his fist into the savages windpipe. I could hear it try to breathe through its mangled throat and Zandor raised his leg and kicked it back into its compatriots and they all went down in a heap. Setting his foot to the face of the savage that had claimed his halberd he wrenched it loose with a grunt. Flames flickered in the corner of my vision and I saw that one of the rough planks that made up the ramp was smoldering.

That's it! Valia's unexpected success had given me the solution to our problem.

"I love you," I said in a sudden lull of the fighting and before they could realise what I was doing I had wormed my way past Zandor and sprinted at the savages.

"Mary!" He screamed after me.

I raised a powerful and angled shield and leaped into the air before powering the main jets to full throttle. I shot through the air like a bullet and I almost closed my eyes. If this didn't work and the savages dissolved my shield then I would plough headfirst into them before being ripped to shreds. My shield entered the area of effect of their magic draining aura and the outer layer of my shield started fraying. Then I slammed into them and the angle of my shield threw them to the side. And then I was past them and soaring into the atrium. It was crawling with savages. They where packed tightly along the ramp but their progress was slowed by the need to carefully place their feet so that they didn't harm their eggs. Javelins whistled past me while I steadied my flight and several struck my shield without doing me any harm. I didn't dare move too close to the ramp in case they threw more projectiles so I positioned myself below the ramp to minimise avenues of attack. Of course, the savages directly below me would be able to throw things at me but I hoped that they would exhaust their projectiles before my shield failed.

Even as I felt objects strike my shield below me I called fire into my hands. I let it build in heat and intensity before I threw it at the underside of the ramp above me.

Sure enough, the savages on the ramp above me started leeching the power from my flames, but not fast enough. Within moments the wood blackened and caught fire. Slowly I drifted along the ramp, setting it ablaze as I went. More objects struck my shield but even more thunked into the wood above me. Or even better, they were flung into the savages beneath and above me. Suddenly they didn't care if they trampled their own eggs, or wounded or killed their compatriots. They were smart enough to understand that an unchecked fire in the caves would be more dangerous to them all, including their eggs, than any collateral damage would be. My power waned continuously from the streams of fire I channelled and in leaps and bounds when my shield absorbed the shock of flung objects. I zipped across the cave to repeat the process on that side. The screams of the savages caught in the inferno I had transformed the ramp into was music to my ears.

The more fires I set, the quicker they would spread. It would also make it more difficult to put them out, if the savages even had access to enough water to do so. It also had the additional benefit that it would trap dozens of savages between fires. They would have to brave the flames to escape or either die by smoke inhalation, burns or fall to their deaths when the supports gave out. Cackling maniacally I soared across the chamber again and set fire to a third section. But I was weakening rapidly. Even the power in my choker was running low and my jet engines where stuttering after their prolonged and heavy usage. I wouldn't be able to stay airborne for much longer. I twisted in the air and shot upward toward my friends. I dipped in below the upper region of the ramp and with a final heave I sent out a swathe of flame and I was spent. My shield failed and something solid crashed into my thigh. I felt the bone snap and I let out a short cry of pain.

"Mary!" Valia wailed above me and I looped around the ramp to see the opening clogged with savages. Zandor had lost his halberd and was wielding one of the savages clubs with both hands. It was clear to me that it was too heavy even for his prodigious strength. It was a miracle that the he still managed to hold the tunnel.

"Up!" Felaan screamed and I surged upward in response.

A series of gunshots followed and the savages blocking the mouth of the tunnel fell. I zipped inside and landed with a scream on my broken femur.

"She's hurt!" Valia screamed and knelt next to me even as she took a shot around Zandor.

"Those where our last shots," Wylah snapped over her shoulder as she slung her rifle and drew her hatchet.

"I just need a few moments to splint my leg," I responded. I tried to summon a telekinetic splint but my eyesight wavered and a spear of pain lanced into my brain. "To weak," I murmured.

A small hand on my shoulder and I felt wonderful magic flow into me. Valia were gifting me of her power like I had taught her. I greedily drank it it until I had enough.

"That's enough, love, save your strength," I gasped out before I dove into myself. It took only a few moments for me to align my femur and wrap the break in a solid latticework of telekinetic power. It hadn't been a clean break but I didn't have time to gather all the bone fragments and piece them all together. Neither could I numb the pain and also retain use of my limb.

"Help me up," I asked of my lover and Valia struggled to pull me onto my feet.

Smoke where starting to fill the tunnels and I coughed lightly. That was going to become a problem. Thinking quickly I reached down and sliced a strip of cloth from my trousers where they were not covered in armour and I quickly doused it with water from my canteen before I tied it around my nose and mouth. Valia saw what I was doing and quickly did the same.

"Slow retreat!" I called out and all of us started to walk backwards. I winced in pain for each step, the edges of my vision darkening. While we walked Valia fashioned more masks out of her blouse and by the end of it the garment was more hole than it was an article of clothing. For once I had no attention to spare to gaze upon the lovely flesh on display. The smoke stung my eyes, blurring my vision, as did the stabs of pain from my broken thigh. The stream of savages had thinned, to my surprise, and Felaan felled the last one with a flourish. We stood in the thickening smoke, all ofus breathing hard.

"Injuries?" Wylah asked as we started to warily retreat again.

"Broken femur," I replied.

"I think my left arm is broken, and more cuts and bruises than I can count," Zandor replied. I noticed for the first time that the giant club he had been using had been replaced by a long but much lighter spear that he used with one hand. The other hung uselessly down his side.

"I might have a concussion, and I'm bleeding freely from my side," Felaan reported with his usual stoicism. "What about you, Wylah?"

"More or less unscathed. Out of bullets."

"Same," Felaan said and Valia and I echoed him.

"We can't make another stand in our current state," Felaan stated.

With a howl a group of savages burst into the tunnel.

"Run!" Zandor barked.

We all obeyed. The tunnel had a sharp incline and I soon found myself at the rear, hobbling along on my broken leg.

"Mary, c'mon!" Felaan yelled over his shoulder.

"Can't," I wheezed. "Broken leg."

"I'll carry her," Zandor yelled and slowed down.

"Don't be foolish," Wylah snapped at him. "Not with a broken arm."

"Just go!" I tried to shout but only managed to get a weak wheeze out.

How hadn't the savages caught up to us yet? I risked a glance over my shoulder and the sight spurred my legs on despite the pain and fatigue. They where just strides behind me.

I turned back around and focused on running. Ahead of us Valia's light spell suddenly revealed a wall. It was a dead end.

"Fuck!" Zandor roared furiously.

That was why the savages hadn't truly tried to run us down. They where herding us here so that we would be backed into a corner, exhausted and easy pickings.

Then I saw Wylah vanish to the right. Three steps later I saw that it wasn't a dead end but a crossing. The tunnel branched off in either direction.
