With a surprised grunt of pain Zandor fell to a knee.

I holstered my weapon angrily, furious that he had made me hurt him.

Yet he still refused to budge from his blocking position. I gritted my teeth and used my magic to shove him to the side and he landed on his shoulder with a breath hissed in between his teeth.

"Put pressure on the wound Zandor, or you'll bleed out."

"No." He retorted adamantly. "You do it. I won't. I'll bleed out if you don't help me." Despite the pain he must have been in his voice was clear.

"Fucking stubborn fool!" I spat out in exasperation. Yet I didn't dare approach him to stymie the bleeding for him. I was sure that he would just wrap those strong arms around me and never let go until I hurt him really bad or killed him.

And I didn't want to do either.

"My life is in your hands now, Marissa," he grinned with wide eyes.

But I hadn't stood idle and I had subtly reached into the wound in his leg with my magic. With a flex of my mind flames spat out of the bloody hole in his muscle, cauterising it from the inside.

He didn't scream. He only gritted his teeth, the muscles on the sides of his neck bulging while he stared into my eyes. I walked past him, giving him a wide berth.

As I had suspected he lunged to grab my ankles but I danced out of his reach.

"Get out of here, you fool. I'm gonna rig this tower to explode and I don't want you to get caught in the blast. Get out of the Groundlands, you're in no condition to fight any more."

"I can still shoot a gun," he protested. "Besides, I'm gonna stay right here. I want you to know that if you blow this tower up then I will die in the blast."

I closed my eyes and took a deep breath before I gave him one final look. I seared the image of him into my memory just in case we never saw each other again.

"I love you," I whispered before I turned my back to him and entered the tower.

"I love you too, Marissa. Now and forever," I heard him mutter behind me.

That familiar phrase sent chills down my spine. That was what mother used to tell mom all the time. Now and forever.

I didn't turn back. It would have undone me if I did. Instead I erected a powerful shield to block the entrance and I tied it directly to the power from the tower. Nothing short of a Skyship would be able to break down that shield from the outside.

The inside of the tower was a storm of magic that ripped and burned across my shield but I used that old trick that mom had used when she was first learning how to shield herself. I made my shield partly permeable to magic and I used the power of the tower to shield myself from its fury. Now that I was inside then I had full control of the power supply grid across the Groundlands. I could shut down individual satellites and I could redirect all of the power into the storage crystals below ground. If I did, then there would be no explosion and Zandor would be safe. But the explosion would decimate the core of the Groundlands and have the added benefit of stopping Varl or one of his mages from restoring the power after I left.

Even though it pained me to do so, I decided to destroy the tower. The artificial ley lines would keep feeding power into the overhead nexus but without the receiving lenses that power would simply spill into the crater, causing further damage and manifest as Wild Magic. Perfect for my plans. But possibly fatal for Zandor if he would refuse to let me help him leave. I didn't have much time to waste. Even though I was rather safe here for as long as I wanted, if I wanted to leave then I had to finish before reinforcements arrived or they would block the entrance. With the power of the tower at my disposal very few Mages could stand against me. But I could only channel so much power at any one time and they could overrun me by sheer numbers. No, I had to work quickly and get the fuck out.

What I wanted to do was simple. I wanted to overload the storage crystals below ground. They were ever expanding, constructed by a spell that was powered by the tower itself. I just had to mess with the ratio of power between the construction spell and the charging spell. If the feeding spell fed more power than the rate of expansion could handle... Big boom. I just had to set the ratio to a proper number so that I would have time to get clear before it all went up in flames. But I might as well enact the second step of my plan first. Without further ado I took the trigger crystal out of my pocket, dropped it on the floor and stomped my heel onto it with a faint crunch. I ground my heel for good measure. I could feel the vibrations in the ground when the munitions magazines exploded and a moment later I heard the thunderous roar of the explosions. I imagined the dome shields protecting them from bombardment containing the blasts, utterly destroying anything and anyone caught inside the shields. Soon the anti-air guns would exhaust their caches and fall silent. Then the Skyships could get really bold. With a few sweeps of my hands I adjusted the power ratios of the tower and I dismissed the shield spell blocking the doors before I stepped back outside. With a wave of my hand I recast the spell and tied it to the power inside. That should stop the Mages from trying to fix what I had done.

Zandor still lay outside and I sighed.

"It's done, Zandor. This place will explode soon. Will you let me help you away from here? There is no point in you dying over nothing!"

He was silent for a long while.

"Fine," he snapped.

I didn't truly trust him at this point but I just couldn't leave the man that I loved to his fate. So I abused my body with more Blood Magic and hoisted him to his feet, propping my shoulder into his armpit and I helped him limp away.

"Those explosions..." Zandor gasped between painful steps. "Your secret weapon?"

"Uh huh," I answered while I strained beneath his bulk.

"And this sickness. Your doing as well?"

"Sort of. Alissa provided me with the poison."

"Fuck. I figured as much. I should have known that something was afoot after that trial. But I dared to hope that you had actually abandoned you wayward path after you killed her. I was so proud of you. I know what she meant to you. I had hoped that the Groundlands had come to mean more to you. I guess I was wrong."

"You were. Alissa was like a mother to me. She took me in after my mothers where killed. By the Skylanders," I pointed out.

"They attacked Utopeia," Zandor retorted angrily.

I didn't respond.

"But I guess that might have been warranted," he finally admitted. "We did lay siege to Dun Traelak first."

"That you did. And that is my point. The Skylanders are always the aggressors."

"Sample size of one," Zandor chuckled deliriously while throwing one of my own favourite quotes back in my face.

I grumbled, but he was correct. The Skylanders had been assholes on the Skylands, but they had only fought a single war since coming Outside.

But that didn't matter. Not anymore. It was done.

We hobbled out of what I hoped to be the blast radius and then we kept going, just to be safe.

And then we were suddenly face to face with Micah. And he saw me.

"You!" He roared and his face turned almost as pink as his hair.

His sword flew into his hand and he advanced on me, flanked by his personal cadre of guards.

I felt Zandor's arm stiffen around me, holding me fast.

"Let go, Zandor! There's no point in letting him kill me now! The damage is done!" I pleaded with him.

"The Groundlands need Micah," he hissed into my ear.

"He's a major part of why I have to do this. Remember how he ordered the deaths of those farmers without a second thought? Think he's about to change?"

The words spilled out of my mouth, Micah rapidly approaching with deadly intent. I felt Zandor tighten his grip.

And then he let go of me.

"Run," I hissed to him and gave him a violent push with my magic.

I drew my revolver and aimed while cocking the hammer. Time slowed down.

Micah wasn't a Mage. He was an old warrior and a general and for some reason he wasn't wearing a firearm.

That was his last mistake. His guards carried rifles though and I felt several powerful impacts on my hastily erected shield. One of them even pierced it but was absorbed by my armour. I realigned my aim, lined up my crystal sights with Micah's broad chest and with a grin I squeezed the trigger. I had longed for this moment ever since he broke my trust in him by having all of those farmers killed.

My weapon bucked and spat fire and a bloody hole was torn in Micah's chest. A perfect heart shot. There were no way he was recovering from that even if he survived the initial traumatic shock. His guards weren't Mages either and I lowered my revolver to my side and fanned the hammer. I had spent countless hours in the last decade on the firing ranges of Dun Traelak. By this point I was a deadeye with my mothers revolver. I fanned the hammer with my off hand and dropped the guards with precision shots. Only one man remained standing, surrounded in a shimmering shield.

"Now you die, traitor!" He spat.

It was the same Mage that had accompanied me when I erected those last two focusing towers. I knew that he wasn't a fighter.

"Your plan has failed! Your allies won't break the shield and the Prowler is joining the fight," he gloated.

That was a rookie mistake. If you're gonna fight, then fight. Don't waste time talking.

While he had been gloating I had prepared a spell in my hand behind my back. He must have known that I was casting a spell but he couldn't know what. I was blocking his magesense even though I sincerely doubted that his range would include me.

I flung my spell and a net of lightning lashed out at him, enveloping his shield. Dozens of tiny, insignificant, impacts on his shield spat sparks. The attack had no possibility to break through but that was not my intention. The feedback from the impacts would distract him by their sheer number and it allowed me to close the range. My sword, my mothers sword, was enchanted for this very purpose. By her own hand. I channelled power into the runes on the hilt and the sigils on the blade burst into fiery life. The shield-cutting spell opened his shield as if it were paper and I reversed my slash into a thrust that pierced his heart. He stared down at the length of sharpened crystal that erupted from his chest in disbelief.

"Don't be an idiot," I snarled into his face. "Though I guess giving you advice is pointless now."

I ripped my sword free with a flourish and the man fell stiffly on his face. I had a moment of calm and I raised my eyes to the sky. The Prowler was rising, gun turrets bristling in a diagnostic pattern. I imagined that it had been a decade since the Skyship had participated in any major battle that was anything more than a skirmish with air pirates. But it was still a force to be reckoned with. A small cloud of Angels rose from the Groundlands to latch onto the hull.

Shit. Boarding parties. The anti-air guns where still unloading and here and there I saw a shield flickering around a Bizhjerai ship while they pummelled the shield protecting the gun nests. When those shields went down the boarding parties could enter the vehicles, crippling them from the inside. A quick head count told me that there where enough Angels to board two vessels. That would leave three Skyships to deal with the Prowler. If the Captain of the Prowler was skilled he might be able to disable one or two Skyships, especially with the support of the anti-air guns.

I considered what I should do and took a hesitant step.

Then the world suddenly ended.


---

## Chapter 26 — CHAPTER TWENTY-SIX Battle in the skies {#chapter-26}

CHAPTER TWENTY-SIX

Battle in the skies

The whole world turned entirely white and then my ears popped with a thunderous sound. I was flung through the air as if a giant had swatted me on the back. I rolled head over heels across the mud before I slammed into something solid. Debris and rocks crashed down all around me. I blinked the dancing spots out of my eyes and tried to regain my feet but failed. The air was filled with dust like a thick fog but a bright light illuminated the mist. The shield tower was gone and only the nexus of magic remained. That was the source of the light. Soon it would start to eat away at the land and Wild Magic would ensue. I'd best be gone. My plan was complete. There was nothing more for me to do here. The rest were up to my allies. I finally managed to stand and I stumbled through the ruined camp. I had been just beyond the blast radius but I had no way of observing the damage that had been caused. Magical power flowed over me in uneven torrents and I drank it in hungrily. I would need it soon.

Mother appeared next to me in the thick magical field and she swivelled her head to take in the destruction. A ferocious grin curled her mouth and she laughed.

"You actually did it, love. You are about to bring the Groundlands down!"

I nodded wearily.

"The battle is not over yet," mother agreed. "I think that I can exist here as long as the magic field is this thick but I don't know how far away from the dagger I can go."

Mutely I drew it and offered it to her. She regarded it for a moment before she shook her head. "You keep it. I'd love to stick around and kill Skylanders with you, but you should go. The Skyships will start bombarding this place after they've dealt with the Prowler."

I nodded in agreement. "They've got boarding parties," I informed mother and she grinned in return.

"Then let us deal with them. That much we can do. Let us defend the Skyships so that they can focus on tearing this place down!"

I smiled at mother and she faded from existence, returning to the dagger until she was needed. I unfolded my wings and gave them a quick look to verify that they hadn't been damaged in the blast before I launched into the air. Even as I rose through the fog I heard the distant firing of bombardment cannons and heard the telltale whistle of shells approaching. I hoped that I wouldn't be struck by one of them.

Luckily I wasn't and the fog of dust lit up from above when the destruction of the Groundlands began. I rose above the fog and saw that nearly a fifth of the Groundlands was a crater. I couldn't see any details from this altitude but I couldn't see a single intact building in the blast radius. Even more buildings had been struck by debris and I could see the faint outlines of the dead and wounded scattered throughout the Groundlands.

I turned my attention from the destruction I had wrought and gazed toward the sky. The first few explosions where already flickering on the shields of the Prowler.

And she was responding in kind. Already the cluster of Angels that clung to her hull were dropping, jets flaring and propelling them toward their enemy. They swooped low to attack from below. The only reasonable way to do it in my opinion. Most of the the rest of the hulls of the hulking Skyships where festooned with cannons and point defence turrets but their bellies had to be made clear to allow for landing and mooring. That was also where the majority of the entrance hatches where located. I took a calculated risk and joined the flight of Angels and hoped that the Bizhjerai ships would not unleash with any anti-personnel munitions to take down the swarm.

But I doubted it. The Angel armour was a relatively recent invention and the Skyships hadn't been retrofitted to repel them since it should have taken some time for the design to propagate to our enemies. Kendall had already made plans for it even though there where no other known users of that style of magic. Apart from the Skylanders, of course.

But they did deploy their own Angels to intercept us. I had never fought another airborne opponent and neither had the Bizhjerai Angels. It would be time to improvise. But first I had to make sure that I didn't die to any friendly fire. I drew my dagger and quickly sawed through the straps that secured the armour to my left shoulder and upper arm before I cut through the fabric of my blouse so that I could rip off the sleeve. The tattoos that Alissa had given me to disguise my Bizhjerai Rune had long since faded since I saw no point in keeping them after my lie had been exposed. The two formations of Angels approached one another and when weapons were raised I channelled power into my Bizhjerai Rune to make it blaze, showcasing my allegiance. The strength and vigour that the Rune imparted filled me and I felt strong enough to take on the world. I threw out my arms and channelled my magic in an expanding shockwave to throw the formation I was part of into disarray. I spun onto my back and drew my revolver and shooting a flurry of shots at the nearby surprised Skylander Angels. Six of them plummeted to the ground, either dead or wounded enough to lose control over their wings. They were too inexperienced to maintain flight with a bullet in their body. There were no way I could reload and remain in the air so I holstered my revolver and drew my sword. It was time for spells and blades to rule the day. I flared my jets and shot through the formation, slicing at whoever got in my way. I needed to get out of the formation before the Bizhjerai Angels started shooting. I cut half a dozen Skylanders but only two of them fell to the ground far below us. I shot through the top of the formation just as the first volley was fired. Skylanders fell from the sky in droves but not without returning fire.

But the Bizhjerai Angels were trained elite warriors. They had been hand picked by Marie and trained mercilessly by their captains. Most of them had at least a small aptitude for magic and they could raise shields of their own. Far fewer Bizhjerai fell yet my heart twisted at the sight. They had died because of me. They wouldn't have been here if it weren't for me. I was the driving force behind this invasion, and now my kinsmen paid for it with their blood. I summoned a big spell with both of my hands and unleashed a caustic cloud into the Skylander ranks. Several of those that inhaled it had to slow down, coughing and rubbing their eyes while they attempted to land. It had been a dangerous move of me since the winds could blow the gas in any direction. But I'd had to do something. Then the two forces clashed together in a mad melee. I hovered above it all, taking magical potshots at the Skylanders whenever an opportunity presented itself. Even though the Bizhjerai where outnumbered, they held their ground. Even though they had not expected to fight while airborne, they managed it better than the Skylanders. Mainly due to their experience at flight. But they were too few in number to tie down all of the Skylander Angels and large groups of them split off from the airborne melee and flew toward the Skyships. I was torn for a moment between helping the airborne fight and defending the Skyships. But the Skyships were more important. If the Skylanders crippled even one of them then the odds would quickly shift. I zoomed after the Skylanders. They were already latching onto the belly of Kendall's flagship, the Red Terror. It had been named after mom's nickname during the war. The Skylander Angels were tearing and cutting at the crystal armour and hatches with specialised tools that I did not recognise. Varl and his Mages must have constructed them for a situation just like this. Within moments the hatches were open and the Skylanders swarmed into the guts of the great crystal behemoth. Almost a third of them remained on the hull when I caught up to them and I raked them with great gouts of fire. They hadn't expected an attack from behind and they died in droves when their panicked screams forced them to inhale the flames. One by one the charred forms fell like a morbid rain toward the plains below. The Prowler glided past on the other side and I heard it unleash its broadside into the unshielded ship but the Fist of Heaven responded almost immediately from the other side. It punished the Prowler's shields with a broadside of its own. But the shields absorbed the volley as if it was nothing. Where the hell where the other Skyships?

I spun in a circle and swore. They were all methodically wiping out the now unshielded anti-air gun nests. It would take them only a handful of moments to utterly destroy them but it would take them several minutes to alter their headings to face the Prowler. And the Prowler would be in their midst, its broadsides aimed at their sterns. It was a masterful, and bold, move of the Skylander captain. To dive deep into the enemy formation the moment he saw a weakness. He would be able to fire at them with impunity for quite a while before they could realign their cannons. There was nothing I could do about it, and so I dove into the Kendall's flagship.

The moment my heeled boots struck the deck mother materialised beside me. Her sword, an ethereal copy of the actual blade by my hip, was already in her hand and an angry crimson spell dripped embers between her fingers.

"Let's kill these assholes," she hissed.

I nodded and drew my revolver to quickly reload it before I shifted it to my left hand and drew my sword. As one we charged into the bowels of the Skyship.

Almost immediately we found the first corpse, a Skylander shot through the head. The next one we found was a Bizhjerai, a crew member that had only been armed with a revolver and completely unarmoured. Mother paused beside the body and knelt down to put her hand on his shoulder.

"I didn't know this boy," she said sadly. "Yet his death pains me. I destroyed the Skylands, killed millions of them, so that no more Clansmen would die to their hands."

"This is my fault, mother. Not yours."

"No," she said while standing up. "This is us finishing what I started. The last remnants of the Skylands dies here today so that the rest of the world can be free. We are still fighting the same war. This boy gave his life to finally end it."

She dashed away without another word and I hastened to follow. We followed the sounds of fighting until we saw the rear lines of the Skylanders. The group was fairly small and I realised that they must have spread out from their point of ingress to cause as much damage as they could. They were likely to go toward the engines and the bridge. If they could disable the former then they would cripple the flagship. If they could take out the latter then they would cripple the fleet. I didn't spare that thought any attention. There where Skylanders to kill.

For the first time in my life I waded into the fray side by side with mother. It was a like a dream come true. I had actually never seen her fight with my own eyes. I had always been sheltered behind thick walls when she went out to risk her life for mine. I had seen her kill, yes, but never fight. It was beautiful. She was like a dancer, flowing smoothly between guards and dodging strikes more often than she blocked or parried. Her movements where precise and calculated with blades often coming within inches of her skin. She knew exactly where her body was, how her balance rested and what type of force the enemies could bring to bear at any moment. I didn't know why she blocked or parried for I doubted that her immaterial form could be damaged by any mundane weapons. But her own weapons where far from ephemeral. They cut as well as my own did. Together we cut and parried, hacked and slashed, weaving shots and spells into our fencing seamlessly.

The Skylanders suddenly found themselves stuck between a line of Bizhjerai marines, a short woman in armour that just wouldn't back down and the ghost of their most feared enemy. They didn't stand a chance. Too soon the last one threw down his weapons and knelt in surrender. Mother drove her sword down his pleading mouth to put an end to him.

The marines stared at mother with wide eyes and then they knelt one by one. Mother inclined her head respectfully before she turned to me.

"More?"

I nodded, and together we hunted.

Two more packs of Skylanders later and we had accrued quite a following of marines and crew to help us repel the boarders.

"You!" I cried and indicated roughly half of our troops. "Secure the engine room and the munitions deck. If they get to them then we have already lost. The rest of you come with me to secure the bridge!"

Quick salutes and then we separated. Mother didn't know the layout of the ship but I had been aboard many a time when I travelled with Uncle Kendall. The closer we got to the bridge the more ferocious was the resistance. Sometimes they anticipated an attack in the rear but there where no Mages among them. Mother and I tore them apart and the marines took care of the rest. Until we reached the bridge. The approach was teeming with Skylander Angels and they had already breached the blast doors that protected the brain of the fleet. Shots pinged out of the bridge and the Skylanders ducked back into the bulkheads for cover.

I gesticulated to mother in the mercenary sign language to convey my plan. She shook her head and gestured back. We were still debating when a tall form stepped into view from the bridge.

It was a tall armoured form, oozing power and lightning as he clutched a tall staff. I would have recognised him anywhere.

"You come onto my ship?" A cultured voice rang out. "My ship? To attack me? I've never been so insulted in my life."

A single shot impacted his powerful shield and he scoffed derisively.

"I'm not a man prone to violence but I have already asked you politely once. Now get off my ship you motherfuckers!" Uncle Kendall roared at them and thrust his staff forward.

A bright light nearly blinded me and a hundred bolts of lightning coursed down the corridor. Power surged from the walls of the Skyship and through him into his spell. He was using the power of the Skyship itself to boost his spell to unbelievable magnitudes. The Skylanders cowered in their cover, but the lightning found them even around the sharp corners. They screamed and smoked and jerked and fell. Nearly half of their number was on the ground, many of them unmoving. The rest lay stunned senseless. The rest of them that had escaped Kendall's fury suddenly didn't seem as eager to anger him.

That's when mother stepped around the corner and revealed herself.

"Leave some to me, old friend," she called out to Kendall.

Even though his helmet hid his face I could see him recoil in surprise. I bounced up to mother and laughed maniacally.

"Have at them, mother!"

The Skylanders stared at her in shock. It was evident that they recognised her. I heard a very distinct and disgusting noise when one of the Skylanders soiled himself in fear.

"That one shat himself, mother," I snickered and pointed at the terrified man.

"Then he is the wisest of them all," mother grinned maliciously. "Leave none alive!" She cried and sprang at the closest Angel.

With a roar of defiance the rest of the marines and the crew joined in and I had to hurry to have a chance to kill Skylanders before the supply ran dry.

They didn't put up much of a fight after our theatrics and Kendall ripped his helmet off even before the last one fell.

"Kyra! Marissa!" He choked out before her gathered us both into a massive embrace.

"Later, Kendall," mother chided him. "We have a battle to win."

Kendall blinked. "Yes, of course."

He spun around and assumed the command podium. "I assumed you secured the engines?"

"I sent a bunch of marines there. They should be able to handle it, or at least keep them occupied until our own Angels return to reinforce them."

"Excellent. They have already crushed the Skylander Angels and are returning. Now, let's see what we can do about the Prowler."

Mother put her hand on my shoulder and I turned to look at her. "I'm running low on power. I think I have to leave soon."

I didn't want her to go, but I understood. "I will summon you when I have access to more power, mother. Thank you."

She pulled me into an embrace, and then she was gone.

"You'll have to explain to me what the fuck is going on here," Kendall muttered without looking at me. "At length," he added pointedly.

"I will, Uncle. I promise."

"I will hold you to that," he said before returning to his duties as Commodore. "Status report," he barked at an ensign.

The young woman turned from her speaking tube to report. "The Prowler has disabled the Fist of Heaven. They aren't dead but their rudders are shot. They will have to withdraw for repairs."

"I expected that," Kendall mused. "Without shields they are very vulnerable. Where is the Prowler now?"

"To our stern, sir."

"And the wind?"

"Starboard prow, sir."

"Perfect. Hard turn to port, if you would be so kind." His head swivelled to address the ensign on the other side. "Ready the port broadsides, signal the others to follow suit if they are able." He turned to yet another officer. "Scry for shield integrity and bring up the illusion screen."

The officer spoke into a speaking tube and without waiting for a reply he cast a spell. An illusory view of the theatre of battle appeared before Kendall and I marvelled at the details. The officer was quite skilled in illusion magic. The Prowler was indeed by our stern, the Fist of Heaven was slowly drifting away from the battle along with one of the smaller Bizhjerai ships.

"Are they retreating?" I asked Kendall.

"Yes. Their shields took a beating so they are pulling back to repair the spells. I'd rather not have them rely on their armour alone."

I agreed. They would be very vulnerable without their shields. The deck shifted beneath my feet and I had to grab the railing of Kendall's podium to keep my footing when the Skyship started turning. The illusory model updated in real time and with a delicate wave of his finger the officer overlaid the various arcs of fire from the broadsides. I watched the blue translucent cones slowly pivot toward the Prowler. The Skylander Skyship was also turning around after its bombardment run and I saw that we would be exchanging broadsides.

"Will we be able to take another pounding?"

His eyes narrowed and he hesitated before he answered. "I think so. I sent down a quick spell design to the shield core that would allow them to tap into the magic that you so helpfully unleashed down there. I doubt that their Captain can do the same," he said arrogantly.

That was one of the reasons that I loved Kendall. He knew his limits, he knew the limits of others and he always managed to have a slight arrogant tone to his words without sounding condescending.

"Mathias Varl might be on the Prowler," I blurted and his head snapped around to gape at me?

"Varl? That old codger still breathes?"

"I was as surprised as you. He fucked up my cover big time when he suddenly showed up with the Prowler."

"Shit. Then they might do the same. That old man taught me, you know?"

I did. Kendall had spoken often of his old master. I knew that he had mixed feelings toward Varl. Varl had been kind to Kendall and had treated him well but the old mans disregard for others had never sat well with the young Kendall.

"Very well," Kendall finally announced. "He has been long overdue meeting with death. Today we shall see if the student has finally become the master."

We waited with bated breath while the Skyships turned. The officer added the Prowlers arcs of fire. We would intersect at almost the same time. Slowly the cones crept closer. But we were turning quicker. We had the wind helping us while it worked against the Prowler.

"Fire," Kendall snapped as soon as all of our guns where in range.

A never ending series of thunder echoed up the crystal halls as all three gunnery decks unloaded two hundred and seventy-five cannons. The illusion gave no indication of any hits.

The overhead light spells flickered when the shield absorbed the Prowler's response.

"Report," Kendall ordered the officer. The man listened to several speaking tubes at once and he started updating the illusion to reflect the reports from the spotters and the scryers.

"Their shield is down, sir, and they have taken significant hull damage."

"Internal damage estimation?"

"Internal structural damage is estimated as minor, sir. Another volley to the same side would cause significantly more damage."

"Gunnery report," Kendall asked the ensign to his right.

"Reloading the cannons sir, two and a half minutes until completion."

"I told you years ago that you should give some more thought to scaling up my automatic rifle turrets," I grumbled.

"Be silent, child," Kendall told me affectionately. He knew that I was correct. If he had retrofitted the ship to use my design then the reloading would be finished much quicker.

"The Prowler will be out of the arcs of fire by the time the reloading has finished, sir," the first woman reported.

"What about the others?"

"They are realigning to bring their cannons to bear but it will take longer than our reloading, sir."

"Tell them to don't bother and focus on the settlement. I want it reduced to rubble. Secure all ships internally before deploying the Angels and the Valkyries south of the wall. I don't want any Skylanders getting away this time. Order them to hold and shoot on sight. No warrior is to leave their post to assault. Keep the Skylanders inside."

"Aye, sir!" She chirped happily and started spewing a barrage of orders into her speaking tubes.

"Keep us turning around, I want to face her head on!" Kendall shouted to the navigations officer.

"What are you thinking?" I asked his suspiciously. If it had been mother in his place then I would have been certain that she meant to ram the Prowler. But that was not Kendall's style.

"You'll see, young one. Watch your elder work his magic. Perhaps you will learn something."

The ship lurched again when we started turning with the wind. After a short while Kendall shouted the command that made me realise his plan.

"Prepare the prow bombardment cannon," he called to the weapons officer. "We're shooting at an airborne target, have them adjust accordingly."

I gaped at him. Bombardment cannons versus a Skyship? That was madness. The bombardment cannon was mounted at the prow to shoot heavy explosive projectiles at a high trajectory to slam into buildings and fortifications. It was so massive that it had to be mounted into the spine of the Skyship so that the main engines could help with compensating for the recoil. Shooting it sideways would twist the Skyship off course or cause it to roll. But that very same mounting meant that it had very little ability to aim. Instead you aimed it mostly by orienting the entire Skyship. Firing that thing at an airborne target that travels perpendicularly...

If anyone could pull off that feat it would be Kendall's crew. These where the best that the Bizhjerai had and they had spent years training. Varl would never see that coming.

"Fire at will, whenever the gunners believe is the opportune moment," Kendall said and clasped his hands behind his back.

I held my breath, waiting. And then I kept waiting. Finally the tremendous roar of the bombardment cannon slammed into me with a thump.

Silence ruled the bridge for a breath. "Report," Kendall whispered.

"Spinal impact," the pretty officer relayed from her speaking tube. "Detonation ripped the Prowler apart. She's dead in the air, sir."

"Excellent, commend the prow gunners. Reduce her to wreckage just to make sure that she does not escape again like she did ten years ago. Then turn us around and let us reduce this awful place to rubble."

Kendall relaxed his posture and turned to face me with a weary smile.

"It would seem that your mad plan worked out, kid."


---

## Chapter 27 — CHAPTER TWENTY-SEVEN Genocide {#chapter-27}

CHAPTER TWENTY-SEVEN

Genocide

"The crew of the Prowler is bailing out, sir," the scrying officer reported suddenly.

Kendall swore. "Deploy the reserve Valkyries to intercept them on the ground," he snapped out before turning around and talking into his own speaking tube. "Love, I need you on the bridge."

"Uncle's here?" I asked, surprised.

"Of course," Kendall scoffed. "He wouldn't let me go without him."

I didn't have time to respond before a man that was slightly shorter than Kendall burst into the bridge at a dead sprint.

"I'm going down there, inspire the troops. The fleet is yours, my love," Kendall told him gently and pressed a soft kiss to his lips.

"Please come back to me," Aurean whispered.

I knew what he was thinking about. Mom's death. She had been wearing the best possible armour that the Bizhjerai could craft. She had been shielding herself with magic. She had been one of the finest warriors of the Clan after years of hard training with mother. Yet she had been wounded, defeated, in the breach at Utopeia. And even though she was being escorted to the healers a stray shot had ended her life in an instant.

Even though the Groundlands were already defeated, a single shot at the wrong time was enough to bring down even the mighty Kendall.

"I will, my love. I promise," Kendall responded softly. "Help me with my armour?"

"Of course."

Kendall's armour started reforming, transforming from the heavy suit that his husband always forced him to wear even though he was protected by the hull and shields of his flagship.

Aurean opened a hidden compartment in the wall and hoisted a compact Angel engine and wing pack.

I raised my eyebrow at Kendall and he shrugged in response.

"In case I rapidly need to go outside. It's faster to have one ready than to materialise it."

I nodded my head at that. It made sense. Kendall's husband attached it to his lover before giving him a ferocious embrace.

"Come, Marissa. Let's end this," Kendall said to me before he walked over to the side of the bridge. He slapped his palm on a glowing segment of wall and a panel slid to the side, revealing a dark hole.

It was the emergency evacuation chute for the bridge crew. "It's quicker this way," Kendall shrugged and then he crossed his arms over his chest, his staff held along his body, and he dropped into the hole.

I shrugged and jumped in after him.

The air whistled in my ears as I fell through the darkness, and then I was suddenly out in the open. Kendall had spread his wings below me, the tails of his coat flaring around his legs dramatically.

I spread my own wings and slowed my descent into a controlled glide. All around me Bizhjerai Valkyries where using their wings to slow their descent and glide toward the bailing crew of the Bizhjerai. I fired my Angel engines to gain some altitude so that I could get a better overview of the battlefield. The southern wall was almost untouched and the cannons were firing at any target they could find. But the Bizhjerai already on the ground where waiting safely beyond their range, patiently waiting for the Skyships to realign to bombard the wall. The Skylanders where trapped. Behind me the Skyship that was southbound powered on, presumably to obliterate the farmsteads and the Skylanders stationed there. Kendall and I joined the Valkyries in their pursuit of the bailing crew.

I drew my sword and revolver and readied myself for a fight.

Surprisingly, it wasn't a fight. The Prowler's crew appeared utterly demoralised and more panicked than defiant. We swooped into their ranks and used the superiour mobility that our Angel armours allowed us and the power of our magic to slaughter them. Kendall laid out vast swathes of destruction with his powerful spells while I settled for more pin-point attacks. I herded the descending Valkyries into tighter clusters that Kendall could blast apart with lightning, fireballs and other nefarious methods of destruction. Only a few scores of them reached the ground but they were not long for this world. The Bizhjerai Valkyries descended on them and ripped them apart in moments.

"Well done, my brothers and sisters!" Kendall called out to them. "Now, go north and join our kin in preparation for the final assault."

With a glance in my direction and a nod he fired his Angel jets and soared northward. I hastened to catch up.

A short while later we dropped down near the front ranks in the centre next to the ground commander of the Bizhjerai troops.

For the second time I was surprised to see Marie's husband in command.

He grinned and reached out to muss my hair when I removed my helmet like he always had when I was a child.

"It's good to see you well and unharmed, Marissa," he said warmly.

"You too, Uncle."

I had a lot of uncles and aunts, something that I had never expected when I was a child on the Skylands and had had only my father around.

I could never had imagined that there where such a generous and warm people in the world that would take in a stray orphan as their own. My heart swelled with love for my people and I felt the tension of years start to fade from my shoulders.

Soon my oath to my mothers would be fulfilled. Only the gruesome task of cleaning up the survivors trapped in the Groundlands remained.

I heard the whistle of incoming shells and I tensed, ready to raise up a shield and throw myself to the ground. But I stopped myself when all of the warriors around me remained relaxed and comfortable.

Dozens of large crates soared toward us from the flagship, single use rockets igniting on their bottoms to slow them down before they ploughed into the ground all along the front ranks.

Immediately the warriors sprang forward, opening them up and discarding the protective plating that promptly started to dissolve into nothing.

Siege mortars and field guns.

I shot Kendall an askance look and he shrugged in response.

"To keep the warriors busy while we wait for them," he said with a lazy gesture at the Skyships.

Within minutes the weapons where assembled and loaded and the gunners started tuning them for range.

"Might as well get started," Kendall muttered before he raised his voice. "Fire at will!"

And the killing started.

With mighty booms the field guns and siege mortars opened up, large explosions tearing the top of the northern wall apart. Crystal fragments and bodies where thrown into the air, yet I felt nothing.

I was more distressed by the sight of the wall that I had constructed being destroyed than the mangled bodies I glimpsed between the embrasures. All along the wall the guns thundered in response but their range was far shorter than the guns that Kendall had brought. A handful of minutes later the gigantic bombardment cannons on his flagship spoke, and the side of the western cliff erupted. That would be one of the gun nests that I had built early on. The avalanche of rubble and shattered rock must have buried my workshop in debris. Again the Skyship fired and another gun nest went up in flames.

Slowly and methodically it destroyed the western cliff defences while it repositioned itself to fire directly into the Groundlands.

It was a sound strategy since the cliff guns would be able to fire down into the Groundlands when we advanced if left intact. The other remaining Skyship was doing the same to the eastern cliff defences, albeit slowly with its smaller bombardment cannon. In the distance I saw the Skyship that had retreated was returning now that there where no threats to its unshielded hull.

A massive explosion behind me made me spin around and I saw the death of the Prowler. It had finally broken apart and its power cores must have ruptured. It was almost entirely wreathed in flames in unnatural colours, fed by the magic bleeding from its cores. The lift compartments had broken, leaking gas, steam and air. The behemoth vessels fell, rapidly picking up speed and leaving a thick trail of smoke. I watched it plummet to the earth and crash into the forest with a titanic boom.

I winced. The forest fire that would undoubtedly rage after such a crash might devastate this whole region.

But the last monument of Skylander might had fallen. The final artifact of the Skylands was no more.

I turned my back on the burning wreckage and hoped that Varl had met his fiery doom inside.

The bombardment from the flagship had stopped and I looked up at it curiously. Why wasn't it using its mighty cannon to destroy the Groundlands?

Then I understood. It was turning to expose its broadside to the city. The bombardment cannon would be gross overkill for anything inside that was not the wall. The broadside would be able to lay down a much more efficient swathe of destruction.

I waited with bated breath until all three Skyships where aligned.

Then the sky alighted with prolonged thunder as hundreds of large guns fired. Purple flames and smoke belched out of the cannons and the tell-tale whistle of shells overpowered the sound of the guns firing. Then the interior of the Groundlands lit up. I couldn't see the destruction from my vantage point, but I didn't need to. I trusted my kinsmen to see this through. My part in this was over, now I was just another pair of boots on the ground.

For hours we waited on the ground and more supplies where periodically dropped from the Skyships. Kendall and I ate in silence while the bombardment continued. The sun rose yet the thick cover of smoke made it seem as if it was twilight.

The ever present thunder of guns made my ears ring and my chest thump with each impact, yet there seemed to be no end to the destruction.

Ten thousand people where a lot, and they covered a large area with housing. One did not simply destroy all of that easily, especially not Skylander dwellings.

But suddenly the entire length of the wall erupted in a series of explosion, and then the front of the wall vanished in smoke and crystal fragments. The gunners where targeting the gun ports and embrasures after having cleared the top of the walls.

To my surprise, the Skylanders gave no sign of surrender. Perhaps there were none left alive in there after that bombardment. If the Skylanders had spent more time refining their Skyships before they assaulted Dun Traelak and relied more on them than infantry then they would have won the war with ease.

Or maybe not, since they couldn't have been able to anticipate the timely arrival of the dragons. But to be honest, neither had we. Malkareth had plotted that one all on his own without telling anyone.

After an hour of bombarding the wall several stretches of it collapsed and then the bombardment suddenly halted.

The deafening silence was almost serene, as if the world had suddenly ended. Only the faint clatter of crystal falling while the debris settled could be heard.

Kendall sighed and I stood up from my seat on the ground.

"Alright lads," he called out, "let's see this through so that we can go home. House-clearing formations, stay withing sight of at least two parties at all times. Take no chances and take no prisoners. There will be no survivors today."

I could hear the sorrow lining his words. He didn't enjoy violence. In fact, he detested it. But he would do what he had to in order to protect his people. He wanted to end this properly like he should have at Utopeia. If they had completely wiped out the Skylanders then this battle would not have been necessary. Alissa would still have been alive.

The Clansmen quickly organised themselves into smaller squads of between a dozen and a score or warriors, most squads containing at least one member with a magesense worth mentioning. That would let them find survivors cowering in ruins or rubble before they could ambush their squads.

As one, the Clan marched toward the ruined city.

Kendall and I led from the front. The field guns and Skyships sustained a light barrage of shells to keep the breaches cleared and the warriors marched confidently forward. Their trust in our gunners were absolute.

Kendall and I paused a few dozen paces from the walls, shields strong and tuned to reflect small arms fire just in case someone was alive inside the shattered embrasures to take shots at us.

None did. I assumed that if there were anyone alive in there then they would not want to call attention to themselves.

The Bizhjerai stormed the breaches and I could hear a few scattered gunshots.

"Contact!" I heard a woman yell and then a moment later the same voice rang out again. "Enemy down!"

I shared a glance with Kendall and he drew a deep breath.

"Jump infantry, over the wall!"

As one two hundred or more Valkyries and Angels leaped the wall and descended on the closest ruins.

I strained with my magesense to find any survivors but only corpses and rubble where within range.

"A dozen Skylanders in that building," Kendall called out and outlined the building in question with an illusion spell.

A squad of Bizhjerai ran toward the building. The Skylanders inside must have heard the order for shots started to ring out through the broken windows and walls. I glimpsed a woman through a hole in the wall of the second story. Her eyes were large and scared and her face was streaked with blood from a nasty cut on her scalp. She aimed a rifle at the approaching Bizhjerai and took aim. I unslung my rifle, swung it up to cradle into my shoulder and I squeezed the trigger in a well practised movement.

The back of her head erupted in gore and she dropped out of sight. That had been enough to let the Bizhjerai advance safely and they slammed their armoured backs into the wall.

"Frag out!" One of them called while lobbing a glowing crystal into the broken door.

The squad hunkered down and covered their ears. A few moments later the spell stone detonated with a loud bang and the windows billowed light and smoke.

"Go go go!" One of the Clansmen urged and the squad trooped inside.

A single gunshot rang and a short while later a Bizhjerai woman leaned out of the window on the top floor, shooting a thumbs up.

And such passed most of the remainder of the day. We slowly advanced through the ruins, clearing the buildings one by one in an ever expanding circle. Behind us there were no living Skylanders.

I was exhausted and ready to pass out standing when we finally reached the relatively untouched northern wall.

It was finally over.

"It's done," Kendall echoed my thoughts.

"So it is," mother agreed from beside me.

I was not even surprised to see her, the magic field was dense enough to cut with a knife. Soon this place would be crawling with Wild Magic.

"I'm the last Skylander," I whispered.

I didn't feel triumph. No relief. Just... Nothing. Empty.

Everything I had worked for for more than half of my life I had now achieved. And I felt nothing. Not even a shred of satisfaction.

Ten thousand Skylanders had died this day. The last remnants of the colonisation effort were gone.

"No," mother protested gently and clasped my shoulder in her crystal hand. "You are Bizhjerai," she asserted vehemently.

I shook my head slowly. "I don't think that I am. Only those that act like Bizhjerai are. This slaughter... It is not the Bizhjerai way. It's the Skylander way. I've made us like them..."

Realisation struck me hard. I had turned these fine Clansmen into something like the Skylanders.

"Sometimes you must do what is necessary," Kendall tried to console me.

"Isn't that how the Skylanders justified their actions?"

Kendall opened his mouth but then shut it again. He had no response.

"No. I'm still a Skylander. I fear that I always will be," I said and felt a single tear roll down my cheek.

Kendall clasped my other shoulder. "Let's go home, kid. You need a good rest. You've earned it."

I nodded mutely and let him guide me back through the corpse-riddled Groundlands.

When we walked by the shattered command post in the centre of the Groundlands I saw the cluster of bikes that my friends and I had used. Oddly enough they where relatively intact although they had all fallen over. But there was one missing.

Zandor's huge monstrosity was absent and I felt a small twinge of hope that he had made it out alive. Perhaps Wylah had as well, I had not seen her for a few days before the attack. Or maybe her mangled body lay buried in the rubble. We wouldn't have time to sift for any bodies, soon this place would be consumed by Wild Magic. We needed to be gone by then.

Outside of the wall the Bizhjerai where mustering and the Skyships was setting down to load the mortars, field guns and warriors. Within the hour we would be on our way to Dun Traelak. Home. But I was no longer sure if that was home anymore. Or if I was even a Bizhjerai in anything but name. What I had done was not honourable. It had not been the Bizhjerai way. It had been genocide.

Mother stopped me with a hand on my shoulder and with a look at Kendall she asked him for privacy.

He nodded and strode purposefully away.

"Don't do this to yourself, daughter. I know what you're thinking, and you might be right. Perhaps you are the last Skylander. But remember what I said before. Being a Skylander is embracing a set of ideas, of methods to achieve your goals."

She let out a deep sigh. "You were Bizhjerai. I still believe that you are. Perhaps you've become a Skylander. But I believe that you can be Bizhjerai again."

"I'm not so sure, mother," I responded heavily and she wrapped her spectral arms around me.

"I know, love. I know. But don't give up. Never give up. You can always become a better person, but not if you give up. I want to believe that I managed to do it. You can do it too."

I squeezed her tight. "Don't go."

"I'm afraid that I don't have a choice," she admitted into my hair. "But as long as you carry me around, I will be here for you. Now and forever."

"Now and forever," I whispered into her bosom.

With a final squeeze of my shoulders she was gone.

I drew a shuddering breath and then I walked into the Skyship.

Time to go home.


---

## Chapter 28 — CHAPTER TWENTY-EIGHT Epilogue {#chapter-28}

CHAPTER TWENTY-EIGHT

Epilogue

One of Kendall's captains had requested to remain to watch over the Wild Magic that had started to form while we loaded up and to keep an eye out for any Skylanders that might have fled north into the valley.

I doubted that they would be able to leave with the Wild Magic plugging the valley mouth but Kendall had allowed it. The rest of us went home.

Months later I found myself standing shielded inside the shield tower of Dun Traelak, staring into the fount that had been the key to winning the war with the Skylands. The place where my brother had hatched. I still missed him dearly. The destruction of the Groundlands had been as much for him as it had been for my mothers, my father, my aunts and uncles. Everyone that had died at the hands of the Skylanders.

I had done it to safeguard the Bizhjerai and the rest of the world from their clutches.

It had been a terrible act. An evil act. But it might have good repercussions. Only the future would be able to judge me.

Even so, I didn't feel at home here among my kin any more. I couldn't feel like a part of the Clan no matter how much I tried.

I could feel mother's presence bloom comfortably around me but she seemed to sense my need to be alone and she did not materialise. She only wrapped me in a psychic embrace and let me think.

I was a Skylander again. But I didn't have to be one for the rest of my life. I had a lot of things to atone for. I would never give up until my final breath.

And I could start by fulfilling my promise to mother. I drew her dagger and stared into the faintly glowing gem. I could feel her attention on what I was doing. I could remove her from the enchantment easily enough. I didn't fear that she would try to fight me like when she had wrested Grasax out of his crystalline prison. She would go gladly into the void to join with mom.

But I felt no eagerness from her. If I decided to do it now, then she would be happy. But if I kept her around with me, she would be fine with that.

I could feel her love for me and her full support for whatever I chose.

I needed her. Would need her advice in the days to come. But she deserved her rest. She deserved a reprieve from her grief and loss.

Selfishly, I put the dagger back in its sheath. I just couldn't let go of her, not after I'd finally had her back.

"Not today, mother. Not today. But one day. You just say the word, and I will let you go. I swear it."

Her presence flared once around me and filling me with warmth before she receded back into her gem. She kept drawing power into herself from the fount, keeping it in reserve for the future.

I caressed the gem with my thumb when the door opened behind me. I already knew who it was.

"Kendall," I greeted my uncle.

"Marissa." He said and stood beside me, magic swirling around his shield. "I thought that I would find you here."

"Why have you come?" I asked him gently.

"I think that you know."

I nodded slowly but didn't respond.

"So you have made your decision then," he said with a heartbroken voice.

The sound of it twisted my heart. "Yes," I whispered. "I can't stay."

Kendall sobbed once before clearing his throat. "I feared that was what you where going to say. Is there anything I can do to change your mind?"

"No," I shook my head sadly. "I'm not Bizhjerai any more. I've lost that privilege."

"You did what you did for the Clan!" Kendall protested angrily, still staring deep into the fount.

"I also did it for me, and for mothers. I could have let the Skylanders be. I could have given them a chance to change. I didn't. I killed them all and in my thirst for vengeance I got fellow Clansmen killed."

"Do you regret it?"

I was silent for a long while. "No. And that is what makes it so bad. That is what is making me Skylander. I can't stay here. The Clansmen look up to me. I can't let my influence convert them into Skylanders. I must leave so that they can remain what they are."

I drew a shuddering breath. "I can't corrupt them further. Mother saw it as well. The war turned them from their nomad ways yet they still retained their heart. They need to be true to what they were, to what they are. I will only hinder that."

"I don't understand," Kendall admitted. "But I will accept your words. What are you going to do?"

"We made a mess of the Groundlands. The magic field will congeal over there and the Wild Magic will spread just like at the Great Geyser. But there is no ocean there protecting the lands from its fury. I have to go there and stop it, somehow."

"And those creatures you told me about. They worry me," Kendall said.

"How so, uncle? They seemed savage and unable of complex thought."

"You told me about their ability to consume magic and that reminded me of the Reavers. So I went to the library when we returned and found an old tome. I believe that these creatures where the precursor to the Reavers, an ancient spell from before the Apocalypse that Grasax used as the basis for the Reavers."

"Why does this worry you so?"

"The manuscript was damaged and there were missing information so I don't have the full picture. I believe that the creatures used to be human. The Mages back then where experimenting wildly with magic and tried to create a race of humans that required no sustenance but magic. It worked, to an extent, but it also reduced the subjects to something less than human. Less intelligent, more aggressive. But the tome was clear on one thing. The humans hunted them to extinction."

"At least one egg must have survived somehow," I concluded.

"As long as there are magic for them to feed on, there will be more."

"Enough to be a threat?"

Kendall shrugged. "Who knows. But I'd rather not find out. But, be careful when you get there. There might be thousands of them now given how dense the magic field is over there. Here, take this gem."

He offered me a gem identical to the one that he had given me so long ago. "Summon me if they are a threat to surrounding lands. We will figure something out."

"You mean kill them all," I said bitterly. I had already turned Kendall into a Skylander it would seem.

"I hope that won't be necessary. I'd rather understand them. Help them. Possibly relocate them somewhere where they can live free without being a threat to anyone."

Perhaps there were still hope in this world.

"I love you, uncle Kendall."

"I love you too, kiddo. When are you leaving?"

I shrugged. "Now is as good a time as any."

Kendall nodded sadly and we left the shield tower together. My bike waited outside and it was already loaded up with provisions.

It was a long ride back to the Groundlands, but it would give me time to think. Kendall opened my saddlebag and pushed a leather bound book into it.

"That's the book I found. I think it will be more useful to you than in the library."

"Thank you, Uncle Kenny," I said and hugged him.

I managed to catch his wan smile when he heard that old nickname that I had used in my youth.

"Anything for you, Marissa. Always. Keep in touch, you hear?"

I nodded into his chest.

"Promise me!"

"I promise."

"And be safe. If I don't hear from you regularly then I will come over there and drag you back here by your ear, you hear?"

"I hear you, uncle," I said and let go.

I looked up into his tear streaked face.

"Say goodbye to everyone for me?"

He nodded, swallowing hard.

"Be safe. I love you."

"I love you too," I said and blinked away unshed tears as I straddled my bike.

With a final long look at the man that had raised me for a decade I started my new bike and rolled through Dun Traelak.

The guards by the inner wall, formerly the outer wall, gave me a curious look but didn't challenge me as I rolled by them. Once outside the road became broader and straighter and I could travel faster. Before I knew it I was out of the city that the fortress had become and I was screaming down the plains.

I didn't know what I would do now apart from heading to the Groundlands to clean up the mess I had made and make it safe for people to resettle there if they wished. Just in case I would need them I had copied all of the blueprints and spells that Kendall had accrued over the years into a power core that was safely secured in my saddle bag. What I would do with it, I did not know.

I wanted to do something that helped people for a change. I was tired of fighting, of subterfuge and betrayal.

I wished to use my skills for good. Building things. Making lives easier.

Magic could do all of that, but it could also cause a lot of harm. Like any tool could.

Perhaps I could teach people that where worthy. Create a cadre of Mages dedicated to the betterment of mankind. To oppose the Skylanders of the world.

Fight fire with fire, so to speak.

Who better to fight the would-be Skylanders of this world than the Last Skylander?
